3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 82.93332pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 48.48058pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 82.93332pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces(\text{local systems on $\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}}$})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 42.82921pt\raise-10.15277pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{j_{!}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})[1]}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 20.02783pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 214.69179pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces(\text{pairs $(V,T)$}).}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 193.57652pt\raise 4.83438pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.83438pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\sim}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 186.23906pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\qed

One can identify non canonically the fiber at
1
of
(\FT\mathcal{F})[-1]
with the vector space underlying
\raisebox{4.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\phi}_{\theta}\mathcal{F}
by the following standard lemma (according to the definition of topological Fourier transformation).

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a perverse sheaf on the neighbourhood of a closed disc
\overline{\Delta}
with no singularity on
\partial\Delta
, and let
\Delta^{>0}
be the union of the open disc
\Delta
and a nonempty open interval in its boundary. Then
\sum\dim\raisebox{4.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\phi}_{\theta-\theta_{i}}\mathcal{F}=\dim\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{c}}(\Delta^{>0},\mathcal{F})
, where the sum is taken over all singular points
\theta_{i}
of
\mathcal{F}
in
\Delta
.∎

Remark 3.4
.

For an object
\mathcal{F}
of
\Perv_{0}^{\mathrm{mon}}
, the canonical morphism
\mathrm{can}:\raisebox{4.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\psi}_{\theta}\mathcal{F}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\raisebox{4.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\phi}_{\theta}\mathcal{F}

from the space of nearby cycles
is an isomorphism (because of
3.2
(
1
)), so we can replace
\raisebox{4.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\phi}_{\theta}
with
\raisebox{4.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\psi}_{\theta}
. However, in order to have compatibility with the tensor structures in
3.2
(
2
), the functor
\raisebox{4.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\phi}_{\theta}
is more convenient.

3.c.
Constant perverse sheaves

The full subcategory
\Perv_{0}^{\mathrm{cst}}
of
\Perv_{0}^{\mathrm{mon}}
consists of objects of
\Perv_{0}
that are constant on
\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}}
. These are the perverse sheaves isomorphic to
j_{!}{\boldsymbol{k}}_{\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}}}^{r}[1]
for some
r\geqslant 0
. The restricted Fourier transformation
j^{-1}\circ\FT
sends
j_{!}{\boldsymbol{k}}_{\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}}}^{r}[1]
to
{\boldsymbol{k}}_{\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}}}^{r}[1]
.

Lemma 3.5
.

The functors

\raisebox{4.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\phi}_{\theta}=\raisebox{4.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\phi}_{\theta,1}:\Perv_{0}^{\mathrm{cst}}\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}\Vect\quad\text{and}\quad\Pi\circ i_{!}:\Vect\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}\Perv_{0}^{\mathrm{cst}}

are mutually inverse equivalences of tensor categories.∎

3.d.
Finite monodromic perverse sheaves

Let
\Perv_{0}^{\widehat{\mu}}
denote the full tensor subcategory of
\Perv_{0}^{\mathrm{mon}}
consisting of objects
\mathcal{F}
for which
there exists a cyclic covering

[m]:\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta_{m}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta}
of degree
m\geqslant 1
, written in coordinates as
\theta_{m}\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}\theta=(\theta_{m})^{m}
, such that
the pullback
[m]^{-1}\mathcal{F}
is an object of
\Perv_{0}^{\mathrm{cst}}
.
An object in
\Perv_{0}^{\widehat{\mu}}
is called a
finite monodromic perverse sheaf
. By specializing Lemma
3.2
, we obtain the following.

Lemma 3.6
.

The functor

\raisebox{4.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\phi}_{\theta}:\Perv_{0}^{\widehat{\mu}}\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}\Vect^{\widehat{\mu}}

is an equivalence of tensor categories.

Proof.

We give details on this proof, as a similar argument will be used for exponential mixed Hodge structures
(see Proposition
5.2
). By the equivalence of Lemma
3.2
(
1
), we can replace
\Perv_{0}^{\widehat{\mu}}
with the category of local systems
\mathcal{L}
on
\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}}
with finite monodromy, and by Remark
3.4
, the functor to
\Vect^{\widehat{\mu}}
is given, on a local system
\mathcal{L}
such that
[m]^{-1}\mathcal{L}
is constant, by
\mathcal{L}\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}(\Gamma(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}},[m]^{-1}\mathcal{L}),G)
, where the action of
G=\mathbf{Z}/m\mathbf{Z}
is that of the Galois group of the covering
[m]:\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}}
.

Conversely, given
(V,G)
in
\Vect^{\widehat{\mu}}
where
G=\mathbf{Z}/m\mathbf{Z}
, we define a local system
\mathcal{L}
with finite monodromy as follows. Let
(\mathcal{V},G)
be the constant local system on
\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}}
with stalk
V
and the induced
G
-action. The local system
[m]_{*}\mathcal{V}
is equipped with the induced
G
-action and the Galois
G
-action. Then
\mathcal{L}
is defined as the invariant subsheaf under the action of
\{(g^{-1},g)\mid g\in G\}
. One checks that both functors are quasi-inverse to each other by using the property that, given
(V,G)
as above, and equipping
{\boldsymbol{k}}^{m}
with the action of
G
induced by the cyclic permutation of the standard basis vectors, then if one equips
V\otimes{\boldsymbol{k}}^{m}
with its natural
G\times G
-action,
(V,G)
is isomorphic to
(V\otimes{\boldsymbol{k}}^{m})^{\mathrm{inv}}
equipped with the induced
G
-action, where
{\mathrm{inv}}
means invariants under the
\{(g^{-1},g)\mid g\in G\}
-action.

One can then express a quasi-inverse functor of the functor
\raisebox{4.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\phi}_{\theta}
of the lemma as follows. Given
(V,G)
in
\Vect^{\widehat{\mu}}
, one first associates with it the object of
\Perv_{0}^{\mathrm{cst}}
with
G
-action defined by
(\Pi(i_{!}V),G)
, and then the object
\mathcal{F}
of
\Perv_{0}^{\widehat{\mu}}
defined as
([m]_{*}\Pi(i_{!}V))^{\mathrm{inv}}
, with the same meaning as above for
{\mathrm{inv}}
.
∎

Remark 3.7
.

Let
[m]:\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta_{m}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{A}^{\!1}
be as above. If
\mathcal{F}=j_{!}\mathcal{L}[1]
belongs to
\Perv_{0}^{\mathrm{mon}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1})
, then

[m]^{-1}\mathcal{F}=[m]^{-1}j_{!}\mathcal{L}[1]=j_{m!}([m]^{-1}\mathcal{L})[1]

also belongs to
\Perv_{0}^{\mathrm{mon}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta_{m}})
.
For an object
\mathcal{F}
in
\Perv_{0}^{\widehat{\mu}}

such that
[m]^{-1}\mathcal{F}
belongs to
\Perv_{0}^{\mathrm{cst}}
,
we can identify
\raisebox{4.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\phi}_{\theta}\mathcal{F}

with the vector space
\raisebox{4.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\phi}_{\theta_{m},1}[m]^{-1}\mathcal{F}
.
The monodromy on the latter space is the identity, but one recovers the action of the monodromy on
\raisebox{4.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\phi}_{\theta}\mathcal{F}
by means of the action of the group of the covering. The drawback with the functor
\mathcal{F}\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}\raisebox{4.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\phi}_{\theta_{m},1}([m]^{-1}\mathcal{F})
is that it is a priori not compatible with the tensor structures. On the other hand, the drawback with the functor
\raisebox{4.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\phi}_{\theta}
of Lemma
3.6
is seen when extending it to mixed Hodge modules. Namely, the part
\raisebox{4.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\phi}_{\theta,\neq 1}
shifts the weights and makes
\raisebox{4.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\phi}_{\theta}
not compatible with tensor product when considering weights. This is the reason for the definition of Section
2
.

4.
Exponential mixed Hodge structures

In this section, we recall the construction due to Kontsevich and Soibelman
[
15
, §4]
of the exponential mixed Hodge structures
\mathrm{H}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}_{\mathrm{c}}(U,f)
and
\mathrm{H}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(U,f)
attached to a regular function
f:U\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{A}^{\!1}
on a smooth quasi-projective variety
U
. We emphasize the weight properties in Proposition
4.7
, together with the pure part
\mathrm{H}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}_{\mathrm{mid}}(U,f)
.

4.a.
A review of exponential mixed Hodge structures

By the Riemann-Hilbert correspondence,
the definitions of
\Perv_{0}
and of the functor
\Pi

can be translated to regular holonomic
{D}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}}
-modules, and, according to
[
15
, §4]
both can be lifted in a compatible way to the category
\MHM(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta})
of mixed Hodge modules on
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta}
. This gives rise to the category
\EMHS
of exponential mixed Hodge structures, which is a full subcategory of
\MHM(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta})
, and there is a projector
\Pi:\MHM(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\EMHS
, which is an exact functor, together with a functorial morphism
N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\Pi(N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})
in
\MHM(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta})
whose kernel and cokernel are constant mixed Hodge modules. Each object of
\EMHS
has a perverse realization in
\Perv_{0}
and the projector
\Pi:\MHM(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\EMHS
realizes as
\Pi:\Perv\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\Perv_{0}
of Section
3
. Each object of
\EMHS
is equipped with a weight filtration in
\EMHS
, defined from that in
\MHM(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta})
by the formula

W^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{EMHS}}_{\ell}\Pi(N)=\Pi(W_{\ell}N)

for any mixed Hodge module
N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
on
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta}
. The category
\EMHS
is equipped with a tensor product, induced by the additive convolution on
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta}
. This product is strictly compatible with the weight filtration
W^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{EMHS}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
. (See loc. cit., or
[
7
, §A.3]
for more details.)

The unit in
\EMHS
is
\Pi\circ{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i_{!}(\mathbf{Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})
, which is pure of weight zero as an object of
\EMHS
. Note that, since
\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},\theta}}:=\mathbf{Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},\theta}}[1]
corresponds to the constant variation of Hodge structure of weight zero on
\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},\theta}
, it has weight one as an object of
\MHM(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},\theta})
and therefore

\Pi\circ{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i_{!}(\mathbf{Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})={}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j_{!}\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},\theta}}

is mixed of weights
\leqslant 1
in
\MHM(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta})
.

Let
N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
be an object of
\MHM(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta})
such that its underlying
{D}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta}}
-module
N
has semi-simple monodromy at the origin. The mixed Hodge structure
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\phi_{\theta}(N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})
, as defined by Saito
[
30
,
31
]
, has a weight filtration
W_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
and a Hodge filtration
F^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}
obtained from that of
N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
as follows (for the weight filtration, we use that the monodromy is semi-simple). We recall that both
\phi_{\theta,1}N
and
\psi_{\theta,\neq 1}N
are obtained by grading
N
with respect to its Kashiwara-Malgrange filtration, so that it is meaningful to speak of a filtration (weight or Hodge) on these vector spaces
induced
by a filtration (weight or Hodge) on
N
.

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

F^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}\psi_{\theta,\neq 1}N
is induced by
F^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}N
and
W_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}(\psi_{\theta,\neq 1}N)=\psi_{\theta,\neq 1}(W_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}+1}N)=\psi_{\theta,\neq 1}(W_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}[-1]N)
,

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

F^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}\phi_{\theta,1}N
is induced by
F^{{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}-1}N=F^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}[-1]N
and
W_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}(\phi_{\theta,1}N)=\phi_{\theta,1}(W_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}N)
.

Remark 4.1
.

Even if
N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}=\Pi(N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})
belongs to
\EMHS
, the weight filtrations
W_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}N
and
W_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{EMHS}}N
may differ, as already seen for
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j_{!}\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},\theta}}
. However, they induce the same filtration on
\phi_{\theta,1}N
and
\psi_{\theta,\neq 1}N
. Indeed, for any
\ell
,
the kernel and cokernel of the natural morphism

W_{\ell}N\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}W_{\ell}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{EMHS}}N
are constant on
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta}
,
hence their vanishing cycle space
\raisebox{4.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\phi}_{\theta}=\raisebox{4.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\phi}_{\theta,1}\oplus\raisebox{4.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\psi}_{\theta,\neq 1}
is zero. Therefore, in the definition above, we can replace
W_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
with
W_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{EMHS}}
and we regard
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\phi_{\theta}
as a functor
\EMHS\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}\MHS
.

4.b.
Weight and irregular Hodge filtrations on the de Rham fiber

We recall here the general framework, for which we refer e.g. to
[
29
, Chap. 3]
. We denote by
\widehat{\theta}
the variable which is Fourier dual to
\theta
. Let
N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
be a mixed Hodge module on
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta}
, with
(N,F^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}N,W_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}N)
as the underlying bifiltered
\mathbf{C}[\theta]\langle\partial_{\theta}\rangle
-module and
(\mathcal{F},W_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathcal{F})
as the underlying filtered
\mathbf{Q}
-perverse sheaf.

Weight filtration

Fourier transformation sends these data to a filtered
\mathbf{C}[\widehat{\theta}]\langle\partial_{\widehat{\theta}}\rangle
-module
(\FT_{\theta}N,W_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\FT_{\theta}N)
, with
W_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\FT_{\theta}N:=\FT W_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}N
, corresponding, via the de Rham functor, to the filtered perverse sheaf
(\FT_{\theta}\mathcal{F},W_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\FT\mathcal{F})
on
\mathbb{A}^{\!1\mathrm{an}}_{\widehat{\theta}}
already considered in Section
3
. Restricting
\FT_{\theta}N
,
resp.

\FT_{\theta}\mathcal{F}
, to
\widehat{\theta}=1
yields the de Rham fiber
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta},N\otimes E^{\theta})
,
resp.
the Betti fiber
(\FT_{\theta}\mathcal{F})_{1}
, which is a finite-dimensional vector space over
\mathbf{C}

resp.

\mathbf{Q}
. Moreover, additive convolution
\star
on
\mathbf{C}[\theta]\langle\partial_{\theta}\rangle
-modules induces tensor product of de Rham fibers.
By exactness of the de Rham fiber functor,
which follows from exactness of
\FT_{\theta}
and the
\mathbf{C}[\widehat{\theta},\widehat{\theta}^{-1}]
-freeness of
j^{+}\FT_{\theta}N
, the natural morphism

\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta},W_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}N\otimes E^{\theta})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta},N\otimes E^{\theta})

is injective and its image is a filtration
W_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta},N\otimes E^{\theta})
. Moreover,
W_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
and
W^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{EMHS}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
have the same de Rham
resp.
Betti image (same argument as for
\raisebox{4.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\phi}_{\theta}
in Remark
4.1
).
We thus have a tensor product behavior of the
W
-filtration analogous to that occurring in Definition
2.7
:

W_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta},(N^{\prime}\star N^{\prime\prime})\otimes E^{\theta})\simeq W_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta},N^{\prime}\otimes E^{\theta})\otimes W_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta},N^{\prime\prime}\otimes E^{\theta}).

Irregular Hodge filtration

On the other hand, the Hodge filtration
F^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}N
gives rise to the Deligne filtration
F_{\mathrm{Del}}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(N\otimes E^{\theta})
(see
[
26
, §6]
), which is indexed by
\mathbf{Z}-A
for some finite subset
A\subset[0,1)\cap\mathbf{Q}
. The natural morphism

\mathrm{H}^{1}\bigl(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta},F_{\mathrm{Del}}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}\DR(N\otimes E^{\theta})\bigr)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta},N\otimes E^{\theta})

is injective (see loc. cit.). Its image is denoted by
F_{\mathrm{irr}}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta},N\otimes E^{\theta})
. The filtration
F_{\mathrm{irr}}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta},N\otimes E^{\theta})
behaves like in Definition
2.7
by additive convolution (see
[
27
, Th. 3.39]
).

Proposition 4.2

(
[
7
, Prop. A.10]
)
.

The functor
N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}\bigl(\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta},N\otimes E^{\theta}),F_{\mathrm{irr}}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}},W_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})
from
\MHM(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta})
to bifiltered vector spaces factors through
\Pi_{\theta}
and any morphism in
\MHM(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta})
(or in
\EMHS
) yields a strictly bifiltered morphism.∎

4.c.
Object of
\EMHS
associated with a regular function

We start by considering a geometric situation, that we will reduce to a question on mixed Hodge modules on
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}
by taking a suitable pushforward. We recall the notation of
[
7
, §A.5]
.

Let
f:U\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta}
be a regular function on a smooth complex quasi-projective variety
U
of dimension
d+1
and let
\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{U}
denote the constant pure Hodge module of rank one and weight
d+1
on
U
, with associated
{D}_{U}
-module
({O}_{U},{\mathrm{d}})
and associated perverse sheaf
\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}_{U}=\mathbf{Q}_{U^{\mathrm{an}}}[d+1]
. For each
r\in\mathbf{Z}
, we consider the mixed Hodge modules in
\MHM(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta})

(\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{U})^{r}_{*}:={H}^{r-d-1}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}f_{*}\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{U},\quad(\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{U})^{r}_{!}:={H}^{r-d-1}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}f_{!}\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{U}.

They define objects

(4.3)

\mathrm{H}^{r}(U,f)=\Pi_{\theta}((\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{U})^{r}_{*})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathrm{H}^{r}_{\mathrm{c}}(U,f)=\Pi_{\theta}((\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{U})^{r}_{!})

of
\EMHS
, and we set

\mathrm{H}^{r}_{\mathrm{mid}}(U,f)=\image[\mathrm{H}^{r}_{\mathrm{c}}(U,f)\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{r}(U,f)]=\image\bigl[\Pi_{\theta}((\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{U})^{r}_{!})\rightarrow\Pi_{\theta}((\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{U})^{r}_{*})\bigr].

More generally, let
N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
be a mixed Hodge module on
U
. We define similarly

(4.4)

(N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{U})^{r}_{*}:={H}^{r-d-1}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}f_{*}N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{U},\quad(N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{U})^{r}_{!}:={H}^{r-d-1}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}f_{!}N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{U},

and

(4.5)

\mathrm{H}^{r}(U,N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},f)=\Pi_{\theta}((N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{U})^{r}_{*})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathrm{H}^{r}_{\mathrm{c}}(U,N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},f)_{\mathrm{c}}=\Pi_{\theta}((N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{U})^{r}_{!}).

Then we set

\mathrm{H}^{r}_{\mathrm{mid}}(U,N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},f)=\image[\mathrm{H}^{r}_{\mathrm{c}}(U,f)\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{r}(U,N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},f)]=\image\bigl[\Pi_{\theta}((N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{U})^{r}_{!})\rightarrow\Pi_{\theta}((N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{U})^{r}_{*})\bigr].

Remark 4.6
.

By definition, the de Rham fiber
\mathrm{H}^{r}_{{\mathrm{dR}},{\mathrm{c}}}(U,N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},f)
,
resp.

\mathrm{H}^{r}_{{\mathrm{dR}}}(U,N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},f)
, is equal to
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{{\mathrm{dR}},{\mathrm{c}}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta},(N_{U})_{!}^{r}\otimes E^{\theta})
,
resp.

\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta},(N_{U})_{*}^{r}\otimes E^{\theta})
, and
\mathrm{H}^{r}_{{\mathrm{dR}},{\mathrm{mid}}}(U,N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},f)
is the image of the former to the latter. Under the isomorphism

\mathrm{H}^{r}_{{\mathrm{dR}},?}(U,N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},f)\simeq\mathrm{H}^{r}_{{\mathrm{dR}},?}(U,N_{U}\otimes E^{f})\quad(?=\varnothing,{\mathrm{c}},{\mathrm{mid}}),

the irregular Hodge filtration on the de Rham fiber of
\mathrm{H}^{r}_{?}(U,N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},f)\in\EMHS
is identified with the irregular Hodge filtration
F_{\mathrm{irr}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathrm{H}^{r}_{{\mathrm{dR}},?}(U,N_{U}\otimes E^{f})
of the right-hand side, computed by means of a compactification of
f
as a map
\overline{U}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}
, and the structure morphism of
\overline{U}
(see
[
28
, Th. 1.3(4)]
and
[
7
, (A.16) & (A.17)]
).

The following weight properties are obtained in a way similar to that of
[
7
, Prop. A.19]
.

Proposition 4.7
.

Assume that
N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
is pure of weight
w+1
. Then the exponential mixed Hodge structure
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{d+1}(U,N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},f)
is mixed of weights
\leqslant w+1
,
\mathrm{H}^{d+1}(U,N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},f)
is mixed of weights
\geqslant w+1
, and
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{mid}}^{d+1}(U,N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},f)
is pure of weight
w+1
. Moreover, if
N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
is self-dual, i.e.,
\boldsymbol{D}N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}(-w-1)\simeq N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
, the following properties are equivalent:

(1)

the natural morphism
\gr_{w+1}^{W}\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{d+1}(U,N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},f)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\gr_{w+1}^{W}\mathrm{H}^{d+1}(U,N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},f)
is an isomorphism,

(2)

the equality

\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{mid}}^{d+1}(U,N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},f)=W_{w+1}\mathrm{H}^{d+1}(U,N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},f)
holds.∎

5.
Finite monodromic exponential mixed Hodge structures

This section continues Section
4
in the case where
U
takes the form
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s}\times V
and
f=s^{m}g
for some
m\geqslant 1
and some regular function
g:V\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{A}^{\!1}
. We first define the category
\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}
of finite monodromic exponential mixed Hodge structure. Then, in this particular case,
\mathrm{H}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}_{?}(U,f)

(
?={\mathrm{c}},\varnothing,{\mathrm{mid}}
)
is an object of
\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}
. We make explicit in geometric terms its classical and non-classical components (Proposition
5.10
). The case where
g
is moreover assumed to be tame is considered in Section
5.e
, where the main objective is to provide tools for computing the irregular Hodge filtration on the corresponding de Rham cohomologies: for the classical component, Corollary
5.22
enables us to directly use results of
[
7
, App.]
, while for the non-classical component, the main tool is provided by Corollary
5.32
.

5.a.
Constant exponential mixed Hodge structures

Let
\EMHS^{\mathrm{cst}}
be the full subcategory of
\EMHS
consisting of objects whose associated
\mathbf{Q}
-perverse sheaf belongs to
\Perv_{0}^{\mathrm{cst}}
. Since constant mixed Hodge modules on
\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}}
are exactly those mixed Hodge modules whose associated perverse sheaf is constant (see
[
37
, Th. 4.20]
), we can as well define
\EMHS^{\mathrm{cst}}
as the full subcategory of
\EMHS
consisting of objects whose restriction to
\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}}
is a constant mixed Hodge module.

Lemma 5.1
.

The functors

{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\phi_{\theta,1}:\EMHS^{\mathrm{cst}}\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}\MHS\quad\text{and}\quad\Pi\circ{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i_{!}:\MHS\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}\EMHS^{\mathrm{cst}}

are mutually quasi-inverse equivalences of tensor categories strictly compatible with weight filtrations.

Proof.

It follows from Lemma
3.5
that
\EMHS^{\mathrm{cst}}
is the essential image of
\Pi\circ\,{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i_{!}
. The remaining statements were already observed in
[
7
, Lem. A.12]
.
∎

5.b.
The pure Hodge module
\mathbb{E}_{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}

Before introducing the category
\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}
, let us consider an example. We keep the notation of Section
3.d
. Let
m
be an integer
\geqslant 2
and let
[m]:\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta_{m}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta}
denote the cyclic ramification of order
m
defined by
\theta_{m}\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}\theta=\theta_{m}^{m}
. Let
\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta_{m}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
be the pure Hodge module of weight
1
on
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta_{m}}
(equivalently, the constant polarized variation of Hodge structure of weight
0
on
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta_{m}}
). The pushforward
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}[m]_{*}\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta_{m}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
decomposes as the direct sum

{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}[m]_{*}\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta_{m}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}=\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\oplus\mathbb{E}_{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}

of two pure Hodge modules of weight
1
on
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}
. We have
\mathbb{E}_{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}={}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j_{*}\,{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j^{*}\mathbb{E}_{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
.
Moreover, the underlying
{D}
-module
j^{*}\mathbb{E}_{m}
is
{O}_{\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}}}
-free of rank
m-1
. The Hodge filtration satisfies
\gr^{p}_{F}\mathbb{E}_{m}=0
for
p\neq 0
. The
\mathbf{Q}
-local system
[m]_{*}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta_{m}}}
decomposes as
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta}}\oplus\mathcal{E}_{m}
. We identify the space of multi-valued global sections of
\mathcal{E}_{m}
with the hyperplane of

\mathbf{Q}^{m}=[m]_{*}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta_{m}}}|_{\{1\}}

defined by “sum of entries equal to zero”, on which the monodromy
\theta\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}e^{2\pi\mathsf{i}\theta}
acts as the automorphism induced by the cyclic permutation of the basis vectors of
\mathbf{Q}^{m}
.

Since no eigenvalue of the monodromy on
\mathcal{E}_{m}
is equal to
1
, the natural morphisms

{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j_{!}\,{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j^{*}\mathbb{E}_{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j_{!*}\,{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j^{*}\mathbb{E}_{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j_{*}\,{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j^{*}\mathbb{E}_{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}

are isomorphisms. Lastly, the space of nearby cycles
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\psi_{\theta,\neq 1}\mathbb{E}_{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
is a pure Hodge structure of rank
m-1
and of weight
0
, with
\gr^{p}_{F}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\psi_{\theta,\neq 1}\mathbb{E}_{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}=0
if
p\neq 0
.

The pure Hodge module
\mathbb{E}_{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
decomposes as a direct sum of pure complex Hodge modules
K_{\zeta}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
of rank one, indexed by the
m
-th roots of unity
\zeta
distinct from
1
,
where the monodromy acts by multiplication by
\zeta
.

On the other hand, we regard
\mathbb{E}_{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
as
\Pi({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}[m]_{*}\,\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta_{m}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})
since
\Pi(\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})=0
, and this object is exponentially pure of weight one.

5.c.
Finite monodromic exponential mixed Hodge structures

We denote by
\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}
the full subcategory of
\EMHS
consisting of objects
N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
such that
\Pi\bigl({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}[m]^{*}N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\bigr)
belongs to
\EMHS^{\mathrm{cst}}
for some finite ramified cyclic covering

[m]:\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta_{m}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta}
. Equivalently, the pullback by
[m]:\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},\theta_{m}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},\theta}
of the flat bundle
j^{*}N
is constant on
\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},\theta_{m}}
.

On
\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}
, the functor
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\psi_{\theta,\neq 1}
takes values in
\MHS({\widehat{\mu}})
by its very definition (see
[
31
]
), where the notation
\MHS({\widehat{\mu}})
is explained in Section
2
. Composing
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\psi_{\theta,\neq 1}
with the equivalence defined by (
2.3
) gives rise to a functor

{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\psi_{\theta,\neq 1}^{\widehat{\mu}}:\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\MHS^{\widehat{\mu}}_{\neq 1}.

For example, for
m\geqslant 2
,
\mathbb{E}_{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
has pure (exponential) weight one and
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\psi_{\theta,\neq 1}^{\widehat{\mu}}\mathbb{E}_{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
has also pure
{\widehat{\mu}}
-weight equal to one.

The vanishing cycle functor
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\phi_{\theta,1}
is not affected by this modification in the presence of the
{\widehat{\mu}}
-action, and the sum of both is denoted by
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\phi_{\theta}^{\widehat{\mu}}
.

Proposition 5.2
.

The functor

{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\phi_{\theta}^{\widehat{\mu}}=({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\phi_{\theta,1}\oplus{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\psi_{\theta,\neq 1}^{\widehat{\mu}}):\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}\MHS^{\widehat{\mu}}

is an equivalence of tensor categories which is strictly compatible with weights.

As a consequence, there is a decomposition
\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}=\EMHS^{\mathrm{cst}}\oplus\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}_{\neq 1}
corresponding to the decomposition of Notation
2.5
.

Definition 5.3

(Classical component)
.

For an object
N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
of
\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}
, its
classical component

N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathrm{cl}}\in\EMHS^{\mathrm{cst}}
corresponds to the mixed Hodge structure
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\phi_{\theta,1}N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
. It is a direct summand of
N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
in
\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}
.

Proof of Proposition
5.2
.

We mimic the definition in the proof of Lemma
3.6
to construct a quasi-inverse functor.
Let
(V^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},G)
be an object of
\MHS({\widehat{\mu}})
, with
G=\mathbf{Z}/m\mathbf{Z}
. We associate with it the object

\bigl({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}[m]_{*}(\Pi({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i_{!}V^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}))\bigr)^{\mathrm{inv}},

where
{\mathrm{inv}}
means the invariant submodule with respect to the action of
G^{-1}\times G^{\prime}
. The proof of the equivalence property is then similar to that of loc. cit. That
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\phi_{\theta}^{\widehat{\mu}}
is compatible with the tensor product of each category is similar to
[
34
, Th. (8.11)]
,
2
2

2

We can apply loc. cit. since the monodromy is finite, hence semi-simple, see
[
4
, Rem. 6.6(ii)]
.
and can also be deduced from the more general result
[
18
, Th. 1.2]
.
∎

Remark 5.4
.

For an object
N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
of
\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}_{\neq 1}
(i.e., having a classical component equal to zero), we have
W_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{EMHS}}N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}=W_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
since the underlying
\mathbf{C}[\theta]\langle\partial_{\theta}\rangle
-module
N
does not have any constant sub-quotient. Together with Remark
4.1
, we conclude that the same property holds for objects of
\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}
. From Propositions
2.8
and
5.2
we conclude that the weight filtration satisfies

W_{\ell}(N^{\prime{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}}\star N^{\prime\prime{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}})=\sum_{i}W_{i}N^{\prime{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}}\star W_{\ell-i}N^{\prime\prime{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}},\quad N^{\prime{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}},N^{\prime\prime{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}}\in\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}.

In particular, if
N^{\prime{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}},N^{\prime\prime{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}}
are pure of respective weights
w^{\prime},w^{\prime\prime}
, then their convolution product is pure of weight
w^{\prime}+w^{\prime\prime}
.

Proposition 5.5
.

Let
N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
be an object of
\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}
and let
\bigl(\phi_{\theta}^{\widehat{\mu}}(N),F^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}_{\widehat{\mu}},W^{\widehat{\mu}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\bigr)
denote the bifiltered vector space underlying the associated
{\widehat{\mu}}
-mixed Hodge structure
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\phi_{\theta}^{\widehat{\mu}}(N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})
. Then there exists a functorial bifiltered isomorphism

\bigl(\phi_{\theta}^{\widehat{\mu}}(N),F^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}_{\widehat{\mu}},W^{\widehat{\mu}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\bigr)\simeq\bigl(\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta},N\otimes E^{\theta}),F^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}_{\mathrm{irr}},W_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\bigr)

compatible with tensor products.

Proof.

Compatibility with tensor products follows from Propositions
4.2
and
5.2
. In order to prove the existence of a bifiltered isomorphism, we can decompose
N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
as
N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathrm{cl}}\oplus N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\neq 1}
. The case of
N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathrm{cl}}
has been explained in
[
7
, Prop. B.5]
. We only consider the case of
N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\neq 1}
. By grading with respect to
W_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
, we can assume it is pure and we are left with comparing the Hodge filtrations. We apply the results explained in
[
29
, §5]
. Since the monodromy of
N_{\neq 1}
around
\theta=0
does not have eigenvalue one, and since
N_{\neq 1}
is monodromic, the same property holds at
\theta=\infty
, and Formula (7) in loc. cit. shows that, for
\alpha\in(0,1)
and
\zeta^{\prime}=\exp(-2\pi\mathsf{i}\alpha)
, we have

\dim\gr_{F_{\mathrm{irr}}}^{p+\alpha}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta},N\otimes E^{\theta})=\dim\gr_{F}^{p}\psi_{1/\theta,\zeta^{\prime}}N.

Let us set
\zeta=\zeta^{\prime-1}=\exp(-2\pi\mathsf{i}a)
with
a=-\alpha\in(-1,0)
. Since
N_{\neq 1}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
is monodromic, we have

\dim\gr_{F}^{p}\psi_{1/\theta,\zeta^{\prime}}N=\dim\gr_{F}^{p}\psi_{\theta,\zeta}N,

according to the computation of
[
33
, Th. 2.2]
. The right-hand side equals
\dim\gr_{F_{\widehat{\mu}}}^{p-a}\psi_{\theta,\zeta}N
, according to (
2.1
), that is,
\dim\gr_{F_{\widehat{\mu}}}^{p+\alpha}\psi_{\theta,\zeta}N
, as was to be proved.
∎

Remark 5.6

(Hodge symmetry in
\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}
)
.

According to Remark
2.4
and Proposition
5.5
, we have, for
N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\in\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}
,

\displaystyle\gr^{W}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta},N\otimes E^{\theta})\simeq\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathsf{p},\mathsf{q}\in\mathbf{Q}\\
\mathsf{p}+\mathsf{q}\in\mathbf{Z}\end{subarray}}[\gr^{W}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta},N\otimes E^{\theta})]^{\mathsf{p},\mathsf{q}},

\displaystyle\dim[\gr^{W}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta},N\otimes E^{\theta})]^{\mathsf{p},\mathsf{q}}=\dim[\gr^{W}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta},N\otimes E^{\theta})]^{\mathsf{q},\mathsf{p}},\quad\mathsf{p},\mathsf{q}\in\mathbf{Q},\;\mathsf{p}+\mathsf{q}\in\mathbf{Z}.

5.d.
Object of
\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}
associated with the function
s^{m}g

In this section, we assume
U=\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s}\times V
with
\dim V=d
and
f=s^{m}g
for some regular function
g:V\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau}
and some
m\geqslant 1
. We furthermore assume that
N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}=\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s}}\boxtimes M_{V}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
for some mixed Hodge module
M_{V}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
on
V
.
We prove a property analogous to that of
[
7
, Th. A.24]
.

Proposition 5.7
.

Under these assumptions, for
?=\varnothing,{\mathrm{c}},{\mathrm{mid}}
and for every
r
, the exponential mixed Hodge structure
\mathrm{H}^{r}_{?}(U,N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},s^{m}g)
as defined by (
4.5
) is an object of
\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}
.

Proof.

It is a matter of proving that
(N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{U})^{r}_{*}
and
(N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{U})^{r}_{!}
of (
4.4
), when restricted to
\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},\theta}
, have no singular point and have finite monodromy. By factoring
f
as
(s^{m}\tau)\circ(\id\times g)
and taking first pushforward by
\id\times g
, we are reduced to the case where
V=\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau}
,
g=\id
and we replace
N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{U}
with a mixed Hodge module
N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}=\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s}}\boxtimes M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
for some mixed Hodge module
M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
on
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau}
. In such a way, we are reduced to proving that the underlying
{D}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta}}
-modules
N^{r}_{*}
and
N^{r}_{!}
have no singular point and finite monodromy on
\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},\theta}
. A duality argument also shows that it is enough to consider
N^{r}_{*}
. Moreover, by factoring through
s\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}t=s^{m}
, we are reduced to considering the pushforward by
f=t\tau
of
K_{\zeta}\boxtimes M
, where
K_{\zeta}
is the rank-one
\mathbf{C}[t]\langle\partial_{t}\rangle
-module corresponding to the Kummer sheaf with eigenvalue
\zeta
satisfying
\zeta^{m}=1
, and
M
is any regular holonomic
\mathbf{C}[t]\langle\partial_{t}\rangle
-module on
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau}
. Note that, if
M
is supported at
\tau=0
, the pushforward is supported at
\theta=0
and the assertion is trivially satisfied. We can thus assume that
M=j_{+}j^{+}M
.

If
\zeta=1
, so that
K_{\zeta}={O}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{t}}
, the assertion has been obtained in the proof of
[
7
, Th. A.24(1)]
. If
\zeta\neq 1
, we are reduced to proving that the multiplicative convolution of
j^{+}K_{\zeta}
with
j^{+}M
has no singular point on
\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},\theta}
and has finite monodromy. We identify
\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},t}\times\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},\tau}
with
\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},\theta}\times\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},\tau}
by the change of variable
\theta=t\tau
, so that the map
f
is the first projection. Then
j^{+}K_{\zeta}\boxtimes j^{+}M
is identified with
j^{+}K_{\zeta}\boxtimes(j^{+}K_{\zeta}^{\scriptscriptstyle\vee}\otimes j^{+}M)
and its
r
-th pushforward by the first projection is equal to
(K_{\zeta})^{d_{r}}
, with
d_{r}=\dim\mathrm{H}^{r}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},\tau},j^{+}K_{\zeta}^{\scriptscriptstyle\vee}\otimes j^{+}M)
.∎

Remark 5.8
.

If one replaces
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s}
with
\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},s}
in Proposition
5.7
, i.e.,
U=\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},s}\times V
, then, by
[
7
, Th. A.24(2)]
for
\zeta=1
and the same argument for
\zeta\neq 1
, one obtains that
\mathrm{H}_{?}^{j}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},s}\times V,N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},f)
belongs to
\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}
.

According to the decomposition
\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}=\EMHS^{\mathrm{cst}}\oplus\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}_{\neq 1}
, we have a decomposition, for
?=\varnothing,{\mathrm{c}},{\mathrm{mid}}
and for every
r\in\mathbf{N}
,

\mathrm{H}^{r}_{?}(U,N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},s^{m}g)=\mathrm{H}^{r}_{?}(U,N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},s^{m}g)_{\mathrm{cl}}\oplus\mathrm{H}^{r}_{?}(U,N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},s^{m}g)_{\neq 1}.

We will make more explicit the terms of this decomposition. We start with a preliminary result. We set (there should be no confusion with Notation
3.1
)

{A}=g^{-1}(0),\quad i:{A}\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}V\quad\text{and}\quad j:V^{*}=V\smallsetminus{A}\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}V

and we denote similarly the corresponding inclusions from
D:=\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s}\times{A}
to
U=\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s}\times V
.

Lemma 5.9
.

Assume that
M_{V}
is supported on
{A}
. Then, for all
r
,

\mathrm{H}^{r}_{?}(U,N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},s^{m}g)=\mathrm{H}^{r}_{?}(U,N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},s^{m}g)_{\mathrm{cl}}\simeq\mathrm{H}^{r}_{?}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{t}\times V,\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{t}}\boxtimes M_{V}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})\quad\text{in }\MHS\simeq\EMHS^{\mathrm{cst}}.

In particular,
these objects are independent of
m
.

Proof.

The assumption implies that
M_{V}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}={}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i_{*}M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{{A}}
for some object
M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{{A}}
of
\MHM({A})
, according to the equivalence
[
31
, (4.2.4)]
. Then, denoting by
a
the structure morphism, we have, for
?=*,!
,

{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}f_{?}N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\simeq{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}f_{?}\,{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i_{*}(\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s}}\boxtimes M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{{A}})\simeq{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}a_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{t}\times{A},?}(\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{t}}\boxtimes M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{{A}})\simeq{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}a_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{t}\times V,?}(\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{t}}\boxtimes M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{V}),

in
\MHM(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta})
, and the result follows.
∎

In order to handle the general case, let us define
V^{*}_{m}
so as to make Cartesian the following diagram:

\textstyle{V^{*}_{m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{g_{m}}
\textstyle{[m]_{V}}
\textstyle{V^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{g}
\textstyle{\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},\sigma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[m]}
\textstyle{\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},\tau}}

where the lower horizontal arrow is defined by
[m](\sigma)=\sigma^{m}
. Then
[m]_{V}:V^{*}_{m}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}V^{*}
is a covering and
V^{*}_{m}
is smooth. Let us set
M_{V^{*}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}={}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j^{*}M_{V}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
and let
M_{V^{*}_{m}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
denote the pullback
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}[m]_{V}^{*}M_{V^{*}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
. We recover
M_{V^{*}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
as the
\mu_{m}
-invariant subobject
M_{V^{*}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}=({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}[m]_{V*}M_{V^{*}_{m}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})^{\mu_{m}}
in
\MHM(V^{*})
.

Proposition 5.10
.

With the above notation and assumptions, for
?=\varnothing,{\mathrm{c}},{\mathrm{mid}}
and every
r
, we have the identifications

(5.10
\,*
)

\mathrm{H}^{r}_{?}(U,N_{U},s^{m}g)_{\neq 1}\simeq\Bigl[\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},[m])\otimes\mathrm{H}^{r-1}_{?}(V^{*}_{m},M_{V^{*}_{m}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})\Bigr]^{\mu_{m}}\quad\in\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}_{\neq 1},

where
\mu_{m}
acts diagonally.
If moreover
M_{V}
has no submodule,
resp.
quotient, supported on
{A}
, then

(5.10
\,**
)

\begin{cases}\begin{aligned} \mathrm{H}^{r}(U,N_{U},s^{m}g)_{\mathrm{cl}}&\simeq\mathrm{H}^{r-1}\bigl(U,{H}^{1}({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i_{*}\,{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i^{!}N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})\bigr),\\
\text{resp.}\kern 3.00003pt\mathrm{H}^{r}_{\mathrm{c}}(U,N_{U},s^{m}g)_{\mathrm{cl}}&\simeq\mathrm{H}^{r+1}_{\mathrm{c}}\bigl(U,{H}^{-1}({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i_{*}\,{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i^{*}N_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})\bigr),\end{aligned}\end{cases}\quad\text{in }\MHS\simeq\EMHS^{\mathrm{cst}},

and so

(
\ref{prop:HrUMf}\,{*}{*}{*}
)

\mathrm{H}^{r}_{?}(U,N_{U},s^{m}g)_{\mathrm{cl}}\simeq\mathrm{H}_{?}^{r}(U,N_{U},sg),

which is independent of
m
.

Proof.

By definition, for
x^{\prime}\!\in\!V^{*}_{m}
, we have
g_{m}(x^{\prime})^{m}\!=\!g([m]_{V}(x^{\prime}))
. We consider the isomorphism

\varphi:\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s^{\prime}}\times V^{*}_{m}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s}\times V^{*}_{m},\quad(s^{\prime},x^{\prime})\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}(s^{\prime}/g_{m}(x^{\prime}),x^{\prime}).

Let us set
f_{m}=f\circ(\id\times[m]_{V}):\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s}\times V^{*}_{m}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{A}^{\!1}
. We have

f_{m}(s,x^{\prime})=s^{m}\cdot g([m]_{V}(x^{\prime}))=(s\cdot g_{m}(x^{\prime}))^{m}\quad\text{and}\quad f_{m}\circ\varphi(s^{\prime},x^{\prime})=s^{\prime m}.

As a consequence, we find in
\MHM(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta})
, for each
r
:

\displaystyle{H}^{r-d-1}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}f_{*}(\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s}}\boxtimes j_{*}M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{V^{*}})

\displaystyle\simeq\Bigl[{H}^{r-d-1}\bigl({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}f_{m*}(\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s}}\boxtimes M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{V^{*}_{m}})\bigr)\Bigr]^{\mu_{m}}

\displaystyle\simeq\Bigl[{H}^{r-d-1}\bigl({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}(f_{m}\circ\varphi)_{*}(\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s^{\prime}}}\boxtimes M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{V^{*}_{m}})\bigr)\Bigr]^{\mu_{m}}

\displaystyle\simeq\Bigl[{H}^{r-d-1}\bigl({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}(s^{\prime m})_{*}(\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s^{\prime}}}\boxtimes M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{V^{*}_{m}})\bigr)\Bigr]^{\mu_{m}}

\displaystyle\simeq\Bigl[{H}^{r-d-1}\bigl({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}(s^{\prime m})_{*}(\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s^{\prime}}})\otimes a_{m*}({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{V^{*}_{m}})\bigr)\Bigr]^{\mu_{m}},

where
a_{m}
is the structure morphism of
V^{*}_{m}
. Note that, on the first line, the action of
\mu_{m}
is due to the ramification
[m]_{V}
only while, on the next lines, owing to
\varphi
, the action is due to the ramification
(s^{\prime},x^{\prime})\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}(s^{\prime m},[m]_{V}(x^{\prime}))
. Since
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}(s^{\prime m})_{*}(\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s^{\prime}}})={H}^{0}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}(s^{\prime m})_{*}(\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s^{\prime}}})
, we deduce

{H}^{r-d-1}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}f_{*}(\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s}}\boxtimes j_{*}M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{V^{*}})\simeq\Bigl[{H}^{0}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}(s^{\prime m})_{*}(\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s^{\prime}}})\otimes\mathrm{H}^{r-1}(V^{*}_{m},M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{V^{*}_{m}})\Bigr]^{\mu_{m}}.

After applying
\Pi_{\theta}
, since
\Pi_{\theta}\bigl({H}^{0}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}(s^{\prime m})_{*}(\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s^{\prime}}})\bigr)\!=\!\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},[m])\!=\!\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},[m])_{\neq 1}
,
we obtain that

(5.11)

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{r}\bigl(U,(\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s}}\boxtimes j_{*}M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{V^{*}}),s^{m}g\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{H}^{r}\bigl(U,(\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s}}\boxtimes j_{*}M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{V^{*}}),s^{m}g\bigr)_{\neq 1}

\displaystyle\simeq\Bigl[\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},[m])\otimes\mathrm{H}^{r-1}(V^{*}_{m},M_{V^{*}_{m}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})\Bigr]^{\mu_{m}}.

Arguing similarly with
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j_{!}M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{V^{*}}
, we find

(5.12)

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{r}\bigl(U,(\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s}}\boxtimes j_{!}M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{V^{*}}),s^{m}g\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{H}^{r}\bigl(U,(\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s}}\boxtimes j_{!}M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{V^{*}}),s^{m}g\bigr)_{\neq 1}

\displaystyle\simeq\Bigl[\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},[m])\otimes\mathrm{H}^{r-1}_{\mathrm{c}}(V^{*}_{m},M_{V^{*}_{m}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})\Bigr]^{\mu_{m}}.

Owing to the exact sequences in
\MHM(V)
:

\displaystyle 0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{H}^{0}({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i_{*}\,{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i^{!}M_{V}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}M_{V}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j_{*}M_{V^{*}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{H}^{1}({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i_{*}\,{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i^{!}M_{V}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0,

\displaystyle 0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{H}^{-1}({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i_{*}\,{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i^{*}M_{V}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j_{!}M_{V^{*}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}M_{V}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{H}^{0}({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i_{*}\,{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i^{*}M_{V}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0,

(
5.10

∗

) follows from Lemma
5.9
together with (
5.11
)
resp.
(
5.12
).

The assumption of the second part of the proposition implies that the above exact sequences are respectively short exact sequences, and we obtain (
5.10

∗
⁣
∗

) according to Lemma
5.9
and to the first equality in (
5.11
)
resp.
(
5.12
). The last statement (

∗
∗
∗

‣
5.10
) only expresses the independence of
m
in the right-hand side of (
5.10

∗
⁣
∗

).
∎

Assume for example that
M_{V}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}=\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}_{V}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
, so that
N_{U}=\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
. Since
\boldsymbol{D}(\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})\simeq\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}_{U}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}(d+1)
and
\boldsymbol{D}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i_{*}\,{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i^{*}\simeq{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i_{*}\,{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i^{!}\boldsymbol{D}
, we obtain the identification in
\MHS
:

\mathrm{H}^{r}(U,s^{m}g)_{\mathrm{cl}}\simeq\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{2d+2-r}(U,{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i_{*}\,{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i^{*}\mathbf{Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{U})^{\scriptscriptstyle\vee}(-d-1)=(\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{2d+2-r}(D,\mathbf{Q}))^{\scriptscriptstyle\vee}(-d-1).

Let
\MHM(\mathbf{Q}(e^{2\pi\mathsf{i}/m}))
be the category of
\mathbf{Q}(e^{2\pi\mathsf{i}/m})
-mixed Hodge modules, that is, where we extend the coefficients of the perverse sheaf components to the field
\mathbf{Q}(e^{2\pi\mathsf{i}/m})
. Let
\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}(\mathbf{Q}(e^{2\pi\mathsf{i}/m}))
be the corresponding category of
\mathbf{Q}(e^{2\pi\mathsf{i}/m})
-exponential mixed Hodge structures. In
\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}(\mathbf{Q}(e^{2\pi\mathsf{i}/m}))
, we can decompose
\mathbb{E}_{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
into rank-one objects (Kummer sheaves):

\mathbb{E}_{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\simeq\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{\zeta\in\mu_{m}\smallsetminus\{1\}}K_{\zeta}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}.

Then we obtain an identification in
\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}(\mathbf{Q}(e^{2\pi\mathsf{i}/m}))
:

(5.13)

\mathrm{H}^{r}_{?}(U,s^{m}g)_{\neq 1}\simeq\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{\zeta\in\mu_{m}\smallsetminus\{1\}}\Bigl(K_{\zeta,\theta}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{r-1}_{?}\bigl(V^{*},{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}g^{*}K_{\zeta^{-1},\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\bigr)\Bigr).

Corollary 5.14
.

For any
m\geqslant 1
, we have, in
\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}_{\neq 1}\simeq\MHS^{\widehat{\mu}}_{\neq 1}
,

\mathrm{H}^{d+1}_{\mathrm{mid}}(U,s^{m}g)_{\neq 1}=W_{d+1}\mathrm{H}^{d+1}(U,s^{m}g)_{\neq 1}.

Proof.

By using Formula (
5.10

∗

) with
M_{V}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}=\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}_{V}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
which has pure weight
d
, the assertion reduces to

\mathrm{H}^{d}_{\mathrm{mid}}(V^{*}_{m},\mathbf{Q})=W_{d}\mathrm{H}^{d}(V^{*}_{m},\mathbf{Q}),

which amounts to the property that
\gr_{d}^{W}\mathrm{H}^{d}_{\mathrm{c}}(V^{*}_{m},\mathbf{Q})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\gr_{d}^{W}\mathrm{H}^{d}(V^{*}_{m},\mathbf{Q})
is bijective. Let
X
be a good compactification of
V^{*}_{m}
. Since the mixed Hodge structure
\mathrm{H}^{d}(V^{*}_{m},\mathbf{Q})
has weights
\geqslant d
and
W_{d}\mathrm{H}^{d}(V^{*}_{m},\mathbf{Q})=\image[\mathrm{H}^{d}(X,\mathbf{Q})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathrm{H}^{d}(V^{*}_{m},\mathbf{Q})]
(see
[
20
, Prop. 4.20]
), it follows that
\mathrm{H}^{d}(X,\mathbf{Q})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\gr_{d}^{W}\mathrm{H}^{d}(V^{*}_{m},\mathbf{Q})
is an isomorphism. Dually,
\gr_{d}^{W}\mathrm{H}^{d}_{\mathrm{c}}(V^{*}_{m},\mathbf{Q})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathrm{H}^{d}(X,\mathbf{Q})
is an isomorphism. Since the canonical map
\mathrm{H}^{d}_{\mathrm{c}}(V^{*}_{m},\mathbf{Q})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathrm{H}^{d}(V^{*}_{m},\mathbf{Q})
factors through
\mathrm{H}^{d}(X,\mathbf{Q})
, the assertion follows.
∎

5.e.
The case of a tame function
g

In order to extend Corollary
5.14
to
\EMHS^{\mathrm{cst}}
(see Corollary
5.22
), we assume that
V
is the affine space
\mathbb{A}^{\!d}
(so that
U=\mathbb{A}^{\!d+1}
) and that
g
is a cohomologically tame function on
V
in the sense of
[
22
]
(e.g.
g
is a convenient non-degenerate polynomial in the sense of Kouchnirenko
[
16
]
).

On the one hand, a consequence of the assumption that
V=\mathbb{A}^{\!d}
is that, if
d\geqslant 2
, when considering the exact sequences in
\EMHS^{{\widehat{\mu}}}
(equivalently, in
\MHS^{{\widehat{\mu}}}
) with middle commutative square (see
[
7
, Ex. A.20]
)

(5.15)

\begin{array}[]{c}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 267.4206pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{d}(\mathbb{A}^{\!d})(-1).}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array}

the maps
(*)
are isomorphisms. We can thus regard
\mathrm{H}^{d+1}_{\mathrm{mid}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!d+1},s^{m}g)
as the image of the right vertical arrow.

On the other hand, the tameness assumption implies that the cohomology modules
{H}^{r}g_{\dagger}{O}_{V}
and
{H}^{r}g_{+}{O}_{V}
are constant
{D}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau}}
-modules for
r\neq 0
, as well as the kernel and cokernel of the natural morphism
{H}^{0}g_{\dagger}{O}_{V}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{H}^{0}g_{+}{O}_{V}
(see
[
22
]
).

We consider the pushforward mixed Hodge modules
{H}^{0}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}g_{!}\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!d}}
and
{H}^{0}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}g_{*}\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!d}}
. We thus have

\Pi_{\tau}({H}^{0}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}g_{!}\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!d}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Pi_{\tau}({H}^{0}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}g_{*}\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!d}}).

Let us set
{H}^{0}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}g_{!*}\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!d}}=\image\bigl[{H}^{0}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}g_{!}\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!d}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{H}^{0}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}g_{*}\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!d}}\bigr]
in
\MHM(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau})
. Since
{H}^{0}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}g_{!}\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!d}}
has weights
\leqslant d
and
{H}^{0}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}g_{*}\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!d}}
has weights
\geqslant d
, we conclude that
{H}^{0}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}g_{!*}\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!d}}
is pure of weight
d
. Furthermore, from the above isomorphism, we deduce that

\Pi_{\tau}({H}^{0}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}g_{!*}\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!d}})=\Pi_{\tau}({H}^{0}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}g_{*}\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!d}}).

We set

(5.16)

M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}=\image\bigl[{H}^{0}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}g_{!*}\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!d}}\rightarrow\Pi_{\tau}({H}^{0}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}g_{*}\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!d}})\bigr].

This is a pure Hodge module on
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau}
of weight
d
with no nonzero constant submodule. Furthermore,
\Pi_{\tau}(M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})=\Pi_{\tau}({H}^{0}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}g_{*}\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!d}})
.

Proposition 5.17
.

Under the previous assumptions and if
d\geqslant 2
, there are isomorphisms for
?=\varnothing,{\mathrm{c}},{\mathrm{mid}}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{d+1}_{?}(\mathbb{A}^{\!d+1},s^{m}g)_{\mathrm{cl}}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{H}^{2}_{?}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},t}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau},\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},t}}\boxtimes M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},t\tau),

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{d+1}_{?}(\mathbb{A}^{\!d+1},s^{m}g)_{\neq 1}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{H}^{2}_{?}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},t}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau},{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j^{*}\mathbb{E}_{m,t}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\boxtimes M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},t\tau).

Lemma 5.18
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a constant holonomic
{D}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau}}
-module. Then, for any
r\in\mathbf{Z}
and
m\geqslant 1
,

\mathrm{H}^{r}_{{\mathrm{dR}},?}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},s}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau},{O}_{\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},s}}\boxtimes\mathcal{M},s^{m}\tau)=0\quad\text{for $?=\varnothing,{\mathrm{c}},{\mathrm{mid}}$}.

Proof.

Let us first consider the classical component
\mathrm{H}^{r}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},t}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau},{O}_{\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},t}}\boxtimes\mathcal{M},t\tau)
.
Recall that, for a holonomic
{D}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau}}
-module
\mathcal{M}
, we have

\mathrm{H}^{r}_{{\mathrm{dR}},?}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},t}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau},{O}_{\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},t}}\boxtimes\mathcal{M},t\tau)\simeq\mathrm{H}^{r-1}_{{\mathrm{dR}},?}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},t},j^{+}\FT_{\tau}(\mathcal{M}))\quad\forall r,\ ?={\mathrm{c}},\varnothing.

Furthermore, if
\mathcal{M}
is constant, then
\mathrm{H}^{r}_{{\mathrm{dR}},{\mathrm{c}}}
and
\mathrm{H}^{r}_{\mathrm{dR}}
vanish for all
r
since
\FT_{\tau}\mathcal{M}
is supported at the origin.

For the non-classical component, we can assume
m\geqslant 2
. By taking pushforward by
s\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}t=s^{m}
, we have an identification

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{r}_{{\mathrm{dR}},?}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},s}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau},{O}_{\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},s}}\boxtimes\mathcal{M},s^{m}\tau)_{\neq 1}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{H}^{r}_{{\mathrm{dR}},?}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},t}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau},j^{+}\mathbb{E}_{m,t}\boxtimes\mathcal{M},t\tau)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{H}^{r-1}_{{\mathrm{dR}},?}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},t},j^{+}\mathbb{E}_{m,t}\otimes j^{+}\FT_{\tau}(\mathcal{M})),

and these cohomologies vanish if
\mathcal{M}
is constant.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.17
.

According to the isomorphisms
(*)
in (
5.15
), we may replace
\mathrm{H}^{d+1}_{?}(\mathbb{A}^{\!d+1},s^{m}g)
with
\mathrm{H}^{d+1}_{?}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},s}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!d},s^{m}g)
. Then, applying the pushforward by
g
, the complexes
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}g_{!}\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!d}}
and
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}g_{*}\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!d}}
reduce to the single mixed Hodge module
M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
up to complexes in
\mathsf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\MHM(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau}))
having constant Hodge modules as cohomologies. Therefore, by Lemma
5.18
, the hypercohomologies of these complexes reduce to
\mathrm{H}^{2}_{?}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},s}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau},\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},s}}\boxtimes M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},s^{m}\tau)
. By applying then the pushforward by
s\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}t=s^{m}
we obtain the desired isomorphisms.
∎

Let us focus on the classical component and set
N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}=\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{t}}\boxtimes M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
, so that
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j^{*}N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}=\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},t}}\boxtimes M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
. The following lemma is mainly
[
7
, Cor. A.31]
plus an argument developed within the proof of
[
7
, Th. 3.2]
. We give details for the sake of completeness.

Proposition 5.19

(
[
7
]
)
.

Assume that
M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
is a pure object of
\MHM(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau})
of weight
w
. Then

\mathrm{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{mid}}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},t}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau},{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j^{*}N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},t\tau)=W_{w+1}\mathrm{H}^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},t}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau},{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j^{*}N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},t\tau).

Proof.

Since
M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
is pure, it can be decomposed as the direct sum
M_{0}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\oplus M_{\mathrm{cst}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\oplus M_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
, where
M_{0}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
is supported at
\tau=0
,
M_{\mathrm{cst}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
is constant, and
M_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
is such that
M_{1}
has no submodule nor quotient module supported at
\tau=0
or equal to a constant module.
We can thus consider separately
M_{0}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
,
M_{\mathrm{cst}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
and
M_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
, the case of
M_{\mathrm{cst}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
being solved trivially since both terms in the lemma are zero in that case, according to Lemma
5.18
.

Let us consider a diagram similar to (
5.15
):

(5.20)

\begin{array}[]{c}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern
