241.0252pt\raise-32.64001pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau},M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})(-1)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array}

The upper and lower leftmost terms are zero for
M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}=M_{0}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
or
M_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
, the upper,
resp.
lower, middle terms have weight
\leqslant w+1
,
resp.

\geqslant w+1
(Proposition
4.7
), the upper rightmost term has weights
\leqslant w
and the lower rightmost term has weights
\geqslant w+2
. In these cases, the assertion of the lemma is thus equivalent to the similar one on
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{t}
:

(5.21)

\mathrm{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{mid}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{t}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau},N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},t\tau)=W_{w+1}\mathrm{H}^{2}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{t}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau},N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},t\tau).

The case
M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}=M_{0}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}

Denoting by
i
the inclusion
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{t}\times\{0\}\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{t}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau}
, there exists a pure Hodge structure
V^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
of weight
w
such that
N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}={}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i_{*}(\raisebox{6.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{p}$}{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{t}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\otimes V^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})
. With the notation of (
4.4
) (with
d=1
here), we find that
(N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})_{*}^{j}=0
for
j\neq 1
, hence
\mathrm{H}^{2}(\mathbb{A}^{\!2},N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},t\tau)=0
in that case, so that both terms are zero in (
5.21
).

The case
M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}=M_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}

It has been treated in the proof of
[
7
, Th. 3.2]
. Let us just sketch it. It follows from
[
7
, Cor. A.31(1)]
that

\mathrm{H}^{2}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{t}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau},N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},t\tau)
is isomorphic to the mixed Hodge structure

\coker[{\mathrm{N}}\colon\psi_{\tau,1}M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\rightarrow\psi_{\tau,1}M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}(-1)].

Purity of the mixed Hodge structure
\mathrm{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{mid}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{t}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau},N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},t\tau)
and inclusion in the subspace
W_{w+1}\mathrm{H}^{2}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{t}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau},N^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},t\tau)
are clear from the weight estimates of Proposition
4.7
. We are reduced to proving equality of the dimensions of the de Rham fibers, that is,

\dim\mathrm{H}^{1}_{{\mathrm{dR}},{\mathrm{mid}}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{t},\FT_{\tau}M)=\dim W_{w+1}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{t},\FT_{\tau}M).

This is obtained in loc. cit. by proving the equality

\dim\mathrm{H}^{1}_{{\mathrm{dR}},{\mathrm{mid}}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{t},\FT_{\tau}M)=\dim\rP_{0},

where
\rP_{0}
is the primitive part of weight
w-1
in
\gr^{W}\psi_{\tau,1}M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
.
∎

From Propositions
5.17
and
5.19
we deduce immediately:

Corollary 5.22
.

Under the previous assumptions on
g
and if
d\geqslant 2
, we have in
\EMHS^{\mathrm{cst}}\simeq\MHS
:

\mathrm{H}^{d+1}_{\mathrm{mid}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!d+1},s^{m}g)_{\mathrm{cl}}=W_{d+1}\mathrm{H}^{d+1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!d+1},s^{m}g)_{\mathrm{cl}},

that is, according to (

∗
∗
∗

‣
5.10
),

∎

\mathrm{H}^{d+1}_{\mathrm{mid}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!d+1},tg)=W_{d+1}\mathrm{H}^{d+1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!d+1},tg).

5.f.
Computation of the non-classical part by additive convolution

We keep the setting of Section
5.e
. We revisit (
5.13
) from the point of view of additive convolution. We consider
\mathbb{E}_{m,t}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}

as a pure Hodge module of weight
1
on
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{t}
. Regarded as a complex Hodge module by extending the scalars,
\mathbb{E}_{m,t}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
is the direct sum of Kummer Hodge modules
K_{\zeta,t}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
(
\zeta^{m}=1
,
\zeta\neq 1
), which are pure complex Hodge modules of weight
1
on
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{t}
and Hodge filtration jumping at
0
only. We continue using Notation
3.1
for
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}
with various coordinates.

A review of additive and middle additive convolutions

We refer to
[
6
, §1.1]
for the following results. Let
M
be a holonomic module on
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau}
. We consider the sum map

(5.23)

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau}

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt\Sum\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sum}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{u}

(5.24)

\displaystyle(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mapstochar\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt\hphantom{\Sum}\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hphantom{\Sum}}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}}u=\tau_{1}+\tau_{2},

and define the additive convolutions
\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}\star_{!}M
and
\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}\star M
as the respective pushforwards
\Sum_{\dagger}(\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}\boxtimes M)
and
\Sum_{+}(\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}\boxtimes M)
. These objects of
\mathsf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}({D}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}})
have cohomology in degree zero only, and the image of the natural morphism
\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}\star_{!}M\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}\star M
is denoted by
\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}\star_{\mathrm{mid}}M
. We have

(5.25)

\FT(\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}\star M)\simeq j_{+}j^{+}\FT(\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}\star M)\quad\text{and}\quad\FT(\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}\star_{\mathrm{mid}}M)\simeq j_{{\dagger}+}j^{+}\FT(\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}\star M).

In particular,
\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}\star M
does not have any nontrivial constant submodule.

Weight properties of additive convolutions

We assume that
M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
is a pure Hodge module of weight
w
on
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau}
. Then the various convolutions
\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\star_{?}M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
(
?=!,\varnothing,{\mathrm{mid}}
) are mixed Hodge modules.

Lemma 5.26
.

The mixed Hodge module
\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\star M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
has weights
\geqslant w+1
, and we have

\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\star_{\mathrm{mid}}M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}=W_{w+1}(\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\star M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}).

Proof.

Since
\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\boxtimes M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
is pure of weight
w+1
, it follows that
\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\star_{!}M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
has weights
\leqslant w+1
,
\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\star M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
has weights
\geqslant w+1
, and so
\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\star_{\mathrm{mid}}M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
is pure of weight
w+1
. From (
5.25
) one sees that the quotient
\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}\star M/\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}\star_{\mathrm{mid}}M
is constant, hence so is the quotient
W_{w+1}\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}\star M/\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}\star_{\mathrm{mid}}M
, which underlies a pure Hodge module of weight
w+1
, hence is a direct summand of
W_{w+1}(\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}\star M)
. Since
\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}\star M
does not have any nontrivial constant submodule, the last assertion follows.
∎

Lemma 5.27
.

The mixed Hodge structure
{H}^{0}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i^{!}(\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\star M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})
has weights
\geqslant w+1
and

W_{w+1}\bigl[{H}^{0}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i^{!}(\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\star M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})\bigr]\simeq\rP_{0}\psi_{\tau,1}(\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\star_{\mathrm{mid}}M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})(-1).

Proof.

Since
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i^{!}
increases weights, it follows that

W_{w+1}[{H}^{0}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i^{!}(\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\star M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})]\simeq W_{w+1}[{H}^{0}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i^{!}(\mathbb{E}_{m,\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\star_{\mathrm{mid}}M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})].

Recall that
{H}^{0}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i^{!}
can be computed as
\coker[{\mathrm{N}}_{\tau}:\psi_{\tau,1}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\psi_{\tau,1}(-1)]
on pure Hodge modules (see e.g.
[
7
, Ex. A.2]
) and that the graded component
\gr_{w+1+\ell}^{W}
(
\ell\geqslant 0
) is isomorphic to the primitive part
\rP_{\ell}(-1)
. The conclusion follows.
∎

A comparison result

Let
M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
be a mixed Hodge module on
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau}
. We aim at computing the irregular Hodge filtration associated with the object of
\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}
defined as

(5.28)

\mathrm{H}^{2}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{t}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau},\mathbb{E}_{m,t}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\boxtimes M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},t\tau)=\mathrm{H}^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},t}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau},{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j^{*}\mathbb{E}_{m,t}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\boxtimes M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},t\tau).

Since we are only interested in the Hodge filtration, we work in the context of complex Hodge modules and we consider the objects
\mathrm{H}^{2}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},t}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau},{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j^{*}K_{\zeta,t}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\boxtimes M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},t\tau)
with
\zeta^{m}=1
and
\zeta\neq 1
.

Proposition 5.29
.

Let
M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
be a mixed Hodge module on
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau}
. Then, for
\varepsilon\in\{1,\dots,m-1\}
and
\zeta=\exp(-2\pi\mathsf{i}\varepsilon/m)
, we have

(5.29
\,*
)

\dim\gr^{p-\varepsilon/m}_{F_{\mathrm{irr}}}\mathrm{H}^{r}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{t}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau},K_{\zeta,t}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\boxtimes M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},t\tau)=\dim\gr^{p}_{F}\bigl[{H}^{r-2}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i^{!}(K_{\zeta^{-1},\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\star M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})\bigr],

and both terms are zero for
r\neq 1,2
.

The above formula is stated in the setting of complex Hodge modules in order to make precise the shift by
-\varepsilon/m
. Once this shift is understood, the proof takes place within the category of mixed Hodge modules and yields the formula for the dimension of the Hodge filtration of (
5.28
).

Proof.

We first explain the identification of the underlying de Rham cohomology spaces. If
M
is supported at the origin, i.e.,
M=i_{+}V
, we have

\mathrm{H}^{r}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},t}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau},j^{+}K_{\zeta,t}\boxtimes i_{+}V,t\tau)\simeq\mathrm{H}^{r-1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},t},j^{+}K_{\zeta,t}\otimes_{\mathbf{C}}V),

which is zero for all
r
since
\zeta\neq 1
. We can thus assume that
M=j_{+}j^{+}M
, so that

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{r}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},t}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau},j^{+}K_{\zeta,t}\boxtimes M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}},t\tau)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{H}^{r}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},t}\times\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},\tau},j^{+}K_{\zeta,t}\boxtimes j^{+}M,t\tau)

\displaystyle\simeq\phi_{\theta}\bigl[{H}^{r-2}(t\tau)_{+}(j^{+}K_{\zeta,t}\boxtimes j^{+}M)\bigr]\quad\text{(Lemma \ref{lem:phitheta})}.

On the one hand, we have seen that
{H}^{r-2}(t\tau)_{+}(j^{+}K_{\zeta,t}\boxtimes j^{+}M)
is a vector bundle with connection having monodromy equal to
\zeta\id
and fiber isomorphic to
\mathrm{H}^{r-1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}},j^{+}K_{\zeta^{-1}}\otimes j^{+}M)
, where
\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}}
is regarded as the torus
\{t\tau=1\}
(end of proof of Proposition
5.7
). Therefore,

\displaystyle\dim\mathrm{H}^{r}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},t}\times\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},\tau},j^{+}K_{\zeta,t}\boxtimes j^{+}M,t\tau)

\displaystyle=\dim\phi_{\theta}\bigl[{H}^{r-2}(t\tau)_{+}(j^{+}K_{\zeta,t}\boxtimes j^{+}M)\bigr]

\displaystyle=\dim\psi_{\theta}\bigl[{H}^{r-2}(t\tau)_{+}(j^{+}K_{\zeta,t}\boxtimes j^{+}M)\bigr]

\displaystyle=\rk{H}^{r-2}(t\tau)_{+}(j^{+}K_{\zeta,t}\boxtimes j^{+}M)

\displaystyle=\dim\mathrm{H}^{r-1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}},j^{+}K_{\zeta^{-1}}\otimes j^{+}M).

It follows that the left-hand side can be nonzero only if
r=1,2
.

On the other hand, let
\delta
be the diagonal embedding
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau}\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau}
, let
\iota
denote the involution
\tau\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}-\tau
on the first factor and set
\gamma=\iota\circ\delta:\tau\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}(-\tau,\tau)
. Note that
\iota^{+}K_{\zeta^{-1},\tau}\simeq K_{\zeta^{-1},\tau}
. The base change formula
[
5
, VI, 8.4]
in the present setting reads

i^{+}\Sum_{+}(K_{\zeta^{-1},\tau}\boxtimes M)\simeq a_{+}\bigl(\gamma^{+}(K_{\zeta^{-1},\tau}\boxtimes M)\bigr)\simeq a_{+}\bigl(\delta^{+}(K_{\zeta^{-1},\tau}\boxtimes M)\bigr),

where
a
is the structure morphism. By definition, the
{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}}
-module underlying
\delta^{+}(K_{\zeta^{-1},\tau}\boxtimes M)
is
K_{\zeta^{-1},\tau}\otimes_{{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}}}^{L}M
. Since the
{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}}
-module underlying
K_{\zeta^{-1},\tau}
is
{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}}(*0)
, it is
{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}}
-flat, from which we deduce that

(5.30)

\delta^{+}(K_{\zeta^{-1},\tau}\boxtimes M)\simeq K_{\zeta^{-1},\tau}\otimes_{{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}}}M\simeq j_{+}(j^{+}K_{\zeta^{-1},\tau}\otimes j^{+}M),

and so

{H}^{r-2}\bigl[i^{+}\Sum_{+}(K_{\zeta^{-1},\tau}\boxtimes M)\bigr]\simeq\mathrm{H}^{r-1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}},j^{+}K_{\zeta^{-1}}\otimes j^{+}M).

We conclude that

\dim\mathrm{H}^{r}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{t}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\tau},K_{\zeta,t}\boxtimes M,t\tau)=\dim{H}^{r-2}\bigl[i^{+}(K_{\zeta^{-1}}\star M)\bigr],\quad r=1,2.

Let us now extend the previous computation by taking into account the Hodge filtrations. For the left-hand side of (
5.29

∗

), we have

(5.31)

\displaystyle\dim\gr^{p-\varepsilon/m}_{F_{\mathrm{irr}}}\mathrm{H}^{r}_{\mathrm{dR}}(

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},t}\times\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m},\tau},j^{+}K_{\zeta,t}\boxtimes j^{+}M,t\tau)

\displaystyle=\dim\gr^{p-\varepsilon/m}_{F_{\widehat{\mu}}}\psi_{\theta,\neq 1}^{\widehat{\mu}}\bigl[{H}^{r-2}(t\tau)_{+}(j^{+}K_{\zeta,t}\boxtimes j^{+}M)\bigr]\quad\text{(Proposition \ref{prop:MHSmfirr})}

\displaystyle=\dim\gr^{p}_{F}\psi_{\theta,\neq 1}\bigl[{H}^{r-2}(t\tau)_{+}(j^{+}K_{\zeta,t}\boxtimes j^{+}M)\bigr]\quad\text{(see\kern 3.00003pt\eqref{eq:mfH})}

\displaystyle=\dim\gr^{p}_{F}\psi_{\theta}\bigl[{H}^{r-2}(t\tau)_{+}(j^{+}K_{\zeta,t}\boxtimes j^{+}M)\bigr]\quad\text{(because $\zeta\neq 1$)}

\displaystyle=\rk\gr^{p}_{F}{H}^{r-2}(t\tau)_{+}(j^{+}K_{\zeta,t}\boxtimes j^{+}M),

by using that, for a mixed Hodge module
\mathcal{N}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
on
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{\theta}
, we have
\dim\gr^{p}_{F}\psi_{\theta}\mathcal{N}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}=\rk\gr^{p}_{F}\mathcal{N}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
. We can compute the rank by restricting by the inclusion
i_{1}:\{\theta=1\}\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}}
. Let
\delta^{\prime}
denote the diagonal embedding
\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}}\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}}\times\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}}
and let
{\mathrm{inv}}
denote the involution
t\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}1/t
of
\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}}
. Then
{\mathrm{inv}}\circ\delta^{\prime}
is the inclusion
\gamma^{\prime}:\{t\tau=1\}\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}}\times\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}}
. We note that
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}{\mathrm{inv}}^{!}K^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\zeta,t}\simeq K^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\zeta^{-1},t}
. Therefore, denoting by
a^{\prime}
the structure morphism of
\{t\tau=1\}
, we have

\displaystyle{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i_{1}^{!}\bigl[{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}(t\tau)_{*}({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j^{*}K^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\zeta,t}\boxtimes{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j^{*}M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})\bigr]

\displaystyle\simeq{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}a^{\prime}_{*}\bigl[{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\gamma^{\prime!}({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j^{*}K^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\zeta,t}\boxtimes{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j^{*}M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})\bigr]\quad\text{(see\kern 3.00003pt\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{MSaito87}{}{}, (4.4.3)]})}

\displaystyle\simeq{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}a^{\prime}_{*}\bigl[{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\delta^{\prime!}({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j^{*}K^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\zeta^{-1},t}\boxtimes{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j^{*}M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})\bigr].

Thus, the left-hand side of (
5.29

∗

) is given by
\dim\gr^{p}_{F}{H}^{r-2}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}a^{\prime}_{*}\bigl[{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\delta^{\prime!}({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j^{*}K^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\zeta^{-1},t}\boxtimes{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j^{*}M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})\bigr]
.

For the right-hand side of (
5.29

∗

), taking the notation as above and noting that
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\iota^{!}K^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\zeta^{-1},\tau}\simeq K^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\zeta^{-1},\tau}
, we obtain

{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i^{!}\bigl[\HSum_{*}(K_{\zeta^{-1},\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\boxtimes M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})\bigr]\simeq{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}a_{*}\bigl[{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\delta^{!}(K^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\zeta^{-1},\tau}\boxtimes M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})\bigr].

It remains to be checked that the natural morphism

{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\delta^{!}(K^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\zeta^{-1},\tau}\boxtimes M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j_{*}\,{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\delta^{\prime!}({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j^{*}K^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}_{\zeta^{-1},\tau}\boxtimes{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}j^{*}M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})

is an isomorphism. It is enough to check that the morphism of the underlying
{D}
-modules is an isomorphism, a property which is provided by (
5.30
).
∎

Corollary 5.32
.

For
M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
defined by (
5.16
), for
\varepsilon\in\{1,\dots,m-1\}
and
\zeta=\exp(-2\pi\mathsf{i}\varepsilon/m)
, we have, for each
p\in\mathbf{Z}
,

\displaystyle\dim\gr^{p-\varepsilon/m}_{F}\mathrm{H}^{d+1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!d+1},s^{m}g)_{\neq 1}

\displaystyle=\dim\gr^{p}_{F}\bigl[{H}^{0}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}i^{!}(K_{\zeta^{-1},\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\star M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}),\bigr]

\displaystyle\dim\gr^{p-\varepsilon/m}_{F}\mathrm{H}^{d+1}_{\mathrm{mid}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!d+1},s^{m}g)_{\neq 1}

\displaystyle=\dim\gr^{p}_{F}\bigl[\rP_{0}\psi_{\tau,1}(K_{\zeta^{-1},\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}\star_{\mathrm{mid}}M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}})(-1)\bigr].

Proof.

The first equality is obtained by applying Proposition
5.29
to
M^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{H}}
together with Proposition
5.17
. For the second one, we apply Lemma
5.27
.
∎

6.
The generalized Airy connection and its symmetric powers

6.a.
The generalized Airy differential equation
\mathrm{Ai}_{n}

Let
n\geqslant 2
be an integer. The Airy differential operator of order
n
on
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{z}
is defined as

(6.1)

\partial_{z}^{n}-z

and the corresponding
\mathbf{C}[z]\langle\partial_{z}\rangle
-module is denoted by
\mathrm{Ai}_{n}=\mathbf{C}[z]\langle\partial_{z}\rangle/\mathbf{C}[z]\langle\partial_{z}\rangle\cdot(\partial_{z}^{n}-z)
.
The classical Airy equation corresponds to
n=2
and the corresponding
\mathbf{C}[z]\langle\partial_{z}\rangle
-module is simply denoted by
\mathrm{Ai}
.
Let us set
E^{x^{n+1}/(n+1)}=\mathbf{C}[x]\langle\partial_{x}\rangle/(\partial_{x}+x^{n})
considered as a holonomic
{D}
-module on the affine line
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{x}
.

Lemma 6.2
.

The
\mathbf{C}[z]\langle\partial_{z}\rangle
-module
\mathrm{Ai}_{n}
is the (negative) Fourier transform of
E^{x^{n+1}/(n+1)}
.
In other words,

\mathrm{Ai}_{n}
can be obtained by means of the diagram

\begin{array}[]{c}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 8.40974pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 14.1002pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{x}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 16.95407pt\raise-17.70924pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\!\!f}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 8.40974pt\raise-17.05379pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.50684pt\raise-16.55818pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\pi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 58.21089pt\raise-16.73663pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 64.26474pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-8.40974pt\raise-22.81084pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 30.31032pt\raise-22.81084pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 58.21089pt\raise-22.81084pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{z}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array}\qquad f(x,z)=\tfrac{1}{n+1}\,x^{n+1}-zx,

as

(
6.2
\,*
)

\begin{split}\mathrm{Ai}_{n}&={H}^{0}\pi_{+}E^{f}\\
&\simeq\coker\Bigl[\mathbf{C}[x,z]\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt(\partial_{x}+(x^{n}-z))\otimes{\mathrm{d}}x\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\partial_{x}+(x^{n}-z))\otimes{\mathrm{d}}x}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}\mathbf{C}[x,z]\otimes{\mathrm{d}}x\Bigr]\end{split}

with
\partial_{z}([x^{\ell}]\otimes{\mathrm{d}}x)=-[x^{\ell+1}]\otimes{\mathrm{d}}x
.

Proof.

The negative Fourier transformation is induced by the isomorphism
\mathbf{C}[x]\langle\partial_{x}\rangle\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbf{C}[z]\langle\partial_{z}\rangle
defined by
x\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}\partial_{z}
,
\partial_{x}\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}-z
. That it can be obtained by (

∗

‣
6.2
) is proved in
[
17
, App. 2, §1]
.
∎

The
\mathbf{C}[z]\langle\partial_{z}\rangle
-module
\mathrm{Ai}_{n}
is a free
\mathbf{C}[z]
-module of rank
n
, with a connection
\nabla
having singularity at
\infty
only. Being the Fourier transform of a
\mathbf{C}[x]\langle\partial_{x}\rangle
-module of rank one, thus irreducible,
\mathrm{Ai}_{n}
is also irreducible. Furthermore, the sheaf of horizontal sections
\mathrm{Ai}_{n}^{\nabla}
is the constant sheaf of rank
n
on
\mathbb{A}^{\!1\mathrm{an}}_{z}
. The differential Galois group of
\mathrm{Ai}_{n}
is (see
[
13
, Th. 4.2.7]
)

\begin{cases}\mathrm{SL}_{n}(\mathbf{C})&\text{if $n$ is odd},\\
\mathrm{Sp}_{n}(\mathbf{C})&\text{if $n$ is even}.\end{cases}

It follows that, for
n
even,
\mathrm{Ai}_{n}
is isomorphic to its dual module. More precisely, we have the following behaviour with respect to duality. We denote by
\mathrm{Ai}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle\vee}
the dual
\mathbf{C}[z]\langle\partial_{z}\rangle
-module. It is isomorphic to
\mathbf{C}[z]\langle\partial_{z}\rangle/((-\partial_{z})^{n}-z)
.

Let us denote by
\iota_{n}:\mathbb{A}^{\!1}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{A}^{\!1}
the isomorphism

(6.3)

\iota_{n}:\begin{cases}\displaystyle z\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}\exp(\pi\mathsf{i}/(n+1))\cdot z&\text{if $n$ is odd},\\
\displaystyle z\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}z&\text{if $n$ is even}.\end{cases}

Lemma 6.4
.

We have
\mathrm{Ai}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle\vee}\simeq\iota_{n}^{+}\mathrm{Ai}_{n}
.

Proof.

If
n
is even, we have
(-\partial_{z})^{n}-z=\partial_{z}^{n}-z
. If
n
is odd,
\mathrm{Ai}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle\vee}
is defined by the operator
\partial_{z}^{n}+z
, while
\iota_{n}^{+}\mathrm{Ai}_{n}
is defined by the operator

\exp(-n\pi\mathsf{i}/(n+1))\partial_{z}^{n}-\exp(\pi\mathsf{i}/(n+1))z=\exp(-n\pi\mathsf{i}/(n+1))(\partial_{z}^{n}+z).\qed

The formal stationary phase formula for the local Fourier transform

{F}_{-}^{\infty,\infty}
(see
[
24
, §5.c]
) applied to
E^{x^{n+1}/(n+1)}
shows the following, setting
w=1/z
and
\widehat{\mathrm{Ai}}_{n}=\mathbf{C}(\!(w)\!)\otimes\mathrm{Ai}_{n}
:

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

The formal connection
\widehat{\mathrm{Ai}}_{n}
is isomorphic to the elementary formal connection

(6.5)

[n]_{+}\big(E^{-nt^{n+1}/(n+1)}\otimes L_{(-1)^{n+1}}\big)\simeq\big([n]_{+}E^{-nt^{n+1}/(n+1)}\big)\otimes L_{\mathsf{i}_{n}},

where

–

[n]
is the finite morphism
t\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}z=t^{n}
,

–

L_{(-1)^{n+1}}
is the rank-one formal regular connection
(\mathbf{C}(\!(t^{-1})\!),{\mathrm{d}}+\frac{(n+1)}{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}t/t)
, corresponding to the rank-one local system on the punctured disc with monodromy
(-1)^{n+1}
,

–

we set
\mathsf{i}_{n}=\exp-\pi\mathsf{i}(n+1)/n
(so that
\mathsf{i}_{2}=\mathsf{i}
) and
L_{\mathsf{i}_{n}}
is the rank-one formal regular connection
(\mathbf{C}(\!(w)\!),{\mathrm{d}}+\frac{(n+1)}{2n}\,{\mathrm{d}}w/w)
, corresponding to the rank-one local system on the punctured disc with monodromy
\mathsf{i}_{n}
.

The isomorphism (
6.5
) is not canonical, as we can replace
\mathsf{i}_{n}
with any
n
-th root of
(-1)^{n+1}
and obtain another isomorphism.

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

The irregularity at infinity of
\mathrm{Ai}_{n}
is equal to
n+1
; its (pure) slope is
(n+1)/n
.

6.b.
Symmetric powers of
\mathrm{Ai}_{n}

We now consider
\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n}
. The preliminary analysis of this object can be done as in
[
7
, §2.1]
for the symmetric powers of the Kloosterman connection. Let us set
\mathcal{P}(n,k)=\{{\boldsymbol{a}}\in\mathbf{Z}_{\geqslant 0}^{n}\mid\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}=k\}
. Then
\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n}
has rank
\#\mathcal{P}(n,k)=\binom{n-1+k}{k}
and has an irregular singularity at
\infty
only.

Lemma 6.6

(Irreducibility)
.

The
\mathbf{C}[z]\langle\partial_{z}\rangle
-module
\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n}
is irreducible.

Proof.

Since the differential Galois group
G\subset\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbf{C})
of
\mathrm{Ai}_{n}
equals
\mathrm{SL}_{n}(\mathbf{C})
or
\mathrm{Sp}_{n}(\mathbf{C})
and any symmetric power of the standard representation of such
G
is irreducible, one obtains that
\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n}
is irreducible.
∎

Set
\zeta_{n}=\exp({2\pi\mathsf{i}}/{n})
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}(n,k)

\displaystyle=\textstyle\bigl\{{\boldsymbol{a}}\in\mathcal{P}(n,k)\mid\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\zeta_{n}^{i}=0\bigr\},\quad S_{n,k}=\#\mathcal{S}(n,k),

\displaystyle{\mathrm{irr}}(n,k)

\displaystyle=\frac{n+1}{n}\cdot\#(\mathcal{P}(n,k)\smallsetminus\mathcal{S}(n,k))=\frac{n+1}{n}\biggl[\binom{n-1+k}{k}-S_{n,k}\biggr].

Lemma 6.7
.

We have

\Sym^{k}\widehat{\mathrm{Ai}}_{n}\simeq(\mathbf{C}^{S_{n,k}}\otimes L_{\mathsf{i}_{n}}^{\otimes k})\oplus\Bigl[\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{{\boldsymbol{a}}\in\mathcal{P}(n,k)\smallsetminus\mathcal{S}(n,k)}E^{-n(\sum a_{i}\zeta_{n}^{i})t^{n+1}/(n+1)}\Bigr]^{\mu_{n}}\otimes L_{\mathsf{i}_{n}}^{\otimes k}.

Furthermore,
{\mathrm{irr}}_{\infty}(\Sym^{k}\widehat{\mathrm{Ai}}_{n})={\mathrm{irr}}(n,k)
.

Proof.

We have

\Sym^{k}\widehat{\mathrm{Ai}}_{n}\simeq\Sym^{k}([n]_{+}E^{-nt^{n+1}/(n+1)})\otimes L_{\mathsf{i}_{n}}^{\otimes k}
, and
\Sym^{k}([n]_{+}E^{-nt^{n+1}/(n+1)})
is the
\mu_{n}
-invariant submodule (where the action is given by
t\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}\exp(2\pi\mathsf{i}/n)t
) of

\displaystyle[n]^{+}\Sym^{k}([n]_{+}E^{-nt^{n+1}/(n+1)})

\displaystyle\simeq\Sym^{k}([n]^{+}[n]_{+}E^{-nt^{n+1}/(n+1)})

\displaystyle=\textstyle\Sym^{k}\bigl(\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{n}E^{-n\zeta_{n}^{i}t^{n+1}/(n+1)}\bigr).

The latter
\mathbf{C}(\!(t^{-1})\!)
-module with connection decomposes as

\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{{\boldsymbol{a}}\in\mathcal{P}(n,k)}E^{-n(\sum a_{i}\zeta_{n}^{i})t^{n+1}/(n+1)}.

The result is obtained by taking the
\mu_{n}
-invariant submodule.
∎

Example 6.8

(The case of
n=2
)
.

In the case of
\mathrm{Ai}=\mathrm{Ai}_{2}
,
\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}
has rank
k+1
and we find:

\Sym^{k}\widehat{\mathrm{Ai}}\simeq\begin{cases}\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{j=0}^{(k-1)/2}([2]_{+}E^{2(2j-k)t^{3}/3})\otimes L_{\mathsf{i}}^{\otimes k}&\text{if $k$ is odd},\\[5.0pt]
L_{\mathsf{i}}^{\otimes k}\oplus\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{j=0}^{k/2-1}\Bigl(([2]_{+}E^{2(2j-k)t^{3}/3})\otimes L_{\mathsf{i}}^{\otimes k}\Bigr)&\text{if $k$ is even}.\end{cases}

In particular,
{\mathrm{irr}}_{\infty}(\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai})=3\left\lfloor(k+1)/2\right\rfloor
and, if
k
is odd,
\Sym^{k}\widehat{\mathrm{Ai}}
is purely irregular.

Corollary 6.9
.

One has

\displaystyle\dim\mathrm{H}_{{\mathrm{dR}},?}^{1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\binom{k+n-1}{k}-\frac{n+1}{n}S_{n,k}\quad\text{for $?=\varnothing,{\mathrm{c}}$},

\displaystyle\dim\mathrm{H}_{{\mathrm{dR}},{\mathrm{mid}}}^{1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n})

\displaystyle=\dim\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n})-\begin{cases}\delta_{n\mathbf{Z}}(k)S_{n,k},&\text{$n$ odd},\\
\delta_{2n\mathbf{Z}}(k)S_{n,k},&\text{$n$ even}.\end{cases}

Proof.

The analogue of the Grothendieck-Ogg-Shafarevich formula and Lemma
6.7
give

\chi_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n})=\rk\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n}-{\mathrm{irr}}(n,k).

By irreducibility of
\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n}
and affinity of
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}
, the left-hand side is
-\dim\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n})
. This yields the first equality for
?=\varnothing
. A duality argument gives the case
?={\mathrm{c}}
. The regular part of
\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n}
at infinity has rank
S(n,k)
and monodromy
\mathsf{i}_{n}^{k}\id=\exp(-k(n+1)\pi\mathsf{i}/n)\id
. We thus have

\dim\mathrm{H}_{{\mathrm{dR}},{\mathrm{mid}}}^{1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n})=\begin{cases}\dim\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n})&\text{if }k(n+1)/n\notin 2\mathbf{Z},\\
\dim\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n})-S(n,k)&\text{if }k(n+1)/n\in 2\mathbf{Z},\end{cases}

which yields the second equality.
∎

Let
f_{k}:\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{z}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!k}_{x}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{A}^{\!1}
be defined by

f_{k}(z,x_{1},\dots,x_{k})=\sum_{i=1}^{k}(\tfrac{1}{n+1}\,x_{i}^{n+1}-zx_{i})

and let
\pi_{k}:\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{z}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!k}_{x}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{z}
denote the projection.
The symmetric group
\mathfrak{S}_{k}
acts as automorphisms on
f_{k}
and
\pi_{k}
by permuting the variables
x_{i}
.

Proposition 6.10
.

We have

\mathrm{Ai}_{n}^{\otimes k}\simeq\pi_{k+}E^{f_{k}}\quad\text{and}\quad\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n}\simeq(\pi_{k+}E^{f_{k}})^{\mathfrak{S}_{k},\chi},

where the latter term is the isotypic component of
\pi_{k+}E^{f_{k}}
under the action of
\mathfrak{S}_{k}
with respect to the sign character
\chi
on
\mathfrak{S}_{k}
.

Sketch.

The second identification follows from the first one. We write

\mathrm{Ai}_{n}^{\otimes k}=\mathop{\textstyle\bigotimes}\displaylimits^{k}\FT_{-}(E^{x^{n+1}/(n+1)}),

which we interpret as the Fourier transform
\FT_{-}
of the
k
-fold additive convolution product of
E^{x^{n+1}/(n+1)}
with itself. We note that
\FT_{-}(E^{x^{n+1}/(n+1)})
and its dual are
{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{z}}
-flat, which makes the computation of the iterated convolution easy (
E^{x^{n+1}/(n+1)}
belongs to the category
\mathsf{P}
of Katz
[
14
]
). The iterated convolution can also be computed as the pushforward by the sum map
(x_{1},\dots,x_{k})\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{k}
of
E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\sum}_{i}x_{i}^{n+1}/(n+1)}
. Then the Fourier transform
\FT_{-}
of this convolution can be expressed as
\pi_{k+}E^{f_{k}}
. In particular,
\pi_{k+}E^{f_{k}}={H}^{0}\pi_{k+}E^{f_{k}}
.
∎

Corollary 6.11
.

For
?=\varnothing,{\mathrm{c}}
, the de Rham cohomologies
\mathrm{H}^{r}_{{\mathrm{dR}},?}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n})
vanish for
r\neq 1
and we have

(6.11
\,*
)

\mathrm{H}^{1}_{{\mathrm{dR}},?}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n})\simeq\mathrm{H}^{k+1}_{{\mathrm{dR}},?}(\mathbb{A}^{\!k+1},E^{f_{k}})^{\mathfrak{S}_{k},\chi}.

Proof.

The first assertion for
\mathrm{H}^{r}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n})
follows from the irreducibility of
\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n}
and
\mathrm{H}^{r}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n})=0
for
r\geqslant 2
as
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}
is affine. Furthermore, Poincaré duality for
{D}_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}}
-modules implies (see (
6.3
) for
\iota_{n}
)

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{r}_{{\mathrm{dR}},{\mathrm{c}}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{H}^{2-r}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle\vee})^{\scriptscriptstyle\vee}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{H}^{2-r}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\iota_{n}^{+}\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n})^{\scriptscriptstyle\vee}\simeq\mathrm{H}^{2-r}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n})^{\scriptscriptstyle\vee},

hence the first assertion holds for
\mathrm{H}^{r}_{{\mathrm{dR}},{\mathrm{c}}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n})
.

The case
?=\varnothing
in (
6.11

∗

) is a consequence of the proposition and of the isomorphism
a_{\mathbb{A}^{\!1}+}\circ\pi_{k+}\simeq a_{\mathbb{A}^{\!k+1}+}
, if
a
denotes the structure morphism. For the case
?={\mathrm{c}}
, we argue by duality, using the isomorphism above for the left-hand side. For the right-hand side, we note that
\mathrm{H}^{k+1}_{{\mathrm{dR}},{\mathrm{c}}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!k+1},E^{f_{k}})
is dual to
\mathrm{H}^{k+1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!k+1},E^{-f_{k}})
, and the latter space is isomorphic to
\mathrm{H}^{k+1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!k+1},E^{f_{k}})
as is seen by using the change of variables (compatible with the action of
\mathfrak{S}_{k}
)

(6.12)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle x_{i}\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}-x_{i},\;z\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}z&\text{if $n$ is even},\\
\displaystyle x_{i}\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}\exp(\pi\mathsf{i}/(n+1))x_{i},\;z\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}\exp(-\pi\mathsf{i}/(n+1))z&\text{if $n$ is odd},\end{cases}

which changes
f_{k}
to
-f_{k}
.
∎

6.c.
The
{\widehat{\mu}}
-exponential mixed Hodge structure on the de Rham cohomology of
\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n}
and
\Sym^{k}\widetilde{\mathrm{Ai}}_{n}

In order to apply the results of Section
5
to
\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n}
,
we first consider a ramification of order
n
on the base affine line.
Consider the
n
-fold ramified cover
[n]:\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{z}
given by
s\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}z=s^{n}
. Let
\widetilde{\pi}:\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{x}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s}
denote the first projection and let

\widetilde{f}=\frac{1}{n+1}\,x^{n+1}-s^{n}x

equipped with the
\mu_{n}
action on the variable
s
.
We define

(6.13)

\widetilde{\mathrm{Ai}}_{n}={H}^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{+}E^{\widetilde{f}}\simeq\widetilde{\pi}_{+}E^{\widetilde{f}}\simeq[n]^{+}\mathrm{Ai}_{n},

where the last isomorphism is compatible with the
\mu_{n}
actions.
Then
\widetilde{\mathrm{Ai}}_{n}
is smooth on
\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{s}
and its formal model at infinity is the pullback by
[n]
of (
6.5
), and we have

\mathrm{Ai}_{n}=([n]_{+}\widetilde{\mathrm{Ai}}_{n})^{\mu_{n}}.

Setting

\widetilde{f}_{k}=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\Bigl(\frac{1}{n+1}\,x_{i}^{n+1}-s^{n}x_{i}\Bigr),

we obtain similarly

\widetilde{\mathrm{Ai}}{}_{n}^{\otimes k}\!\simeq\!\widetilde{\pi}_{k+}E^{\widetilde{f}_{k}},\quad\Sym^{k}\widetilde{\mathrm{Ai}}_{n}\!\simeq\!(\widetilde{\pi}_{k+}E^{\widetilde{f}_{k}})^{\mathfrak{S}_{k},\chi},\quad\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n}\!\simeq\!(\Sym^{k}\widetilde{\mathrm{Ai}}_{n})^{\mu_{n}}\!\simeq\!(\pi_{k+}E^{\widetilde{f}_{k}})^{\mu_{n}\times\mathfrak{S}_{k},\chi}.

Corollary 6.14
.

For
?=\varnothing,{\mathrm{c}}
, the de Rham cohomologies
\mathrm{H}^{r}_{{\mathrm{dR}},?}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\widetilde{\mathrm{Ai}}_{n})
and
\mathrm{H}^{r}_{{\mathrm{dR}},?}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}},j^{+}\Sym^{k}\widetilde{\mathrm{Ai}}_{n})
vanish for
r\neq 1
and we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{1}_{{\mathrm{dR}},?}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\widetilde{\mathrm{Ai}}_{n})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{H}^{k+1}_{{\mathrm{dR}},?}(\mathbb{A}^{\!k+1},E^{\widetilde{f}_{k}})^{\mathfrak{S}_{k},\chi},

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{1}_{{\mathrm{dR}},?}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}},j^{+}\Sym^{k}\widetilde{\mathrm{Ai}}_{n})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{H}^{k+1}_{{\mathrm{dR}},?}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!k},E^{\widetilde{f}_{k}})^{\mathfrak{S}_{k},\chi}.

Proof.

We first claim that
\Sym^{k}\widetilde{\mathrm{Ai}}_{n}
does not have any non trivial constant submodule: otherwise its pushforward by
[n]
would give rise, by taking
\mu_{n}
-invariants, to a non trivial constant submodule of
\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n}
, a contradiction. Then we can argue as in the proof of Corollary
6.11
.
∎

As a consequence, we obtain:

\mathrm{H}^{1}_{{\mathrm{dR}},?}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n})\simeq\mathrm{H}^{k+1}_{{\mathrm{dR}},?}(\mathbb{A}^{\!k+1},E^{\widetilde{f}_{k}})^{\mu_{n}\times\mathfrak{S}_{k},\chi}.

The isomorphisms (
6.11

∗

) together with (
4.3
) lead us to define, for
?=\varnothing,{\mathrm{c}},{\mathrm{mid}}
, the exponential mixed Hodge structures
\mathrm{H}_{?}^{1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\mathrm{Ai}_{n}^{\otimes k})
and
\mathrm{H}_{?}^{1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\widetilde{\mathrm{Ai}}{}_{n}^{\otimes k})
as

(6.15)

\mathrm{H}_{?}^{1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\mathrm{Ai}_{n}^{\otimes k}):=\mathrm{H}_{?}^{k+1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!k+1},f_{k}),\quad\mathrm{H}_{?}^{1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\widetilde{\mathrm{Ai}}{}_{n}^{\otimes k}):=\mathrm{H}_{?}^{k+1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!k+1},\widetilde{f}_{k})

and then the exponential mixed Hodge structures
\mathrm{H}_{?}^{1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n})
and
\mathrm{H}_{?}^{1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\widetilde{\mathrm{Ai}}_{n})
as

(6.16)

\mathrm{H}_{?}^{1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n}):=\mathrm{H}_{?}^{1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\mathrm{Ai}_{n}^{\otimes k})^{\mathfrak{S}_{k},\chi},\quad\mathrm{H}_{?}^{1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\widetilde{\mathrm{Ai}}_{n}):=\mathrm{H}_{?}^{1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\widetilde{\mathrm{Ai}}{}_{n}^{\otimes k})^{\mathfrak{S}_{k},\chi},

so that

(6.17)

\mathrm{H}_{?}^{1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n})\simeq\mathrm{H}_{?}^{1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\widetilde{\mathrm{Ai}}_{n})^{\mu_{n}}.

We can similarly define
\mathrm{H}_{?}^{1}(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}},j^{+}\Sym^{k}\widetilde{\mathrm{Ai}}_{n})
in
\EMHS
by replacing
\mathbb{A}^{\!k+1}
with
\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}}\times\mathbb{A}^{\!k}
. Recall that
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{{\mathrm{mid}}}
is defined as the image in
\EMHS
of
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{{\mathrm{c}}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathrm{H}^{1}
.

Theorem 6.18
.

The exponential mixed Hodge structures

\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n}),\quad\mathrm{H}^{1}_{{\mathrm{c}}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n}),\quad\text{and}\quad\mathrm{H}^{1}_{{\mathrm{mid}}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n})

are
{\widehat{\mu}}
-exponential mixed Hodge structures of weights
\geqslant k+1
,
\leqslant k+1
, and
k+1
respectively, and the natural morphisms between them are morphisms of
{\widehat{\mu}}
-exponential mixed Hodge structures.

Moreover, the induced morphism

(6.19)

\gr^{W}_{k+1}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{{\mathrm{c}}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\gr^{W}_{k+1}\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n})

is an isomorphism, and
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{{\mathrm{mid}}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n})
is equal to its image.
This
{\widehat{\mu}}
-exponential pure Hodge structure of weight
k+1
is equipped with a
(-1)^{k+1}
-symmetric pairing

(6.20)

\mathrm{H}^{1}_{{\mathrm{mid}}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n})\otimes\mathrm{H}^{1}_{{\mathrm{mid}}}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\Sym^{k}\mathrm{Ai}_{n})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbf{Q}(-k-1).

Similar properties hold for
\Sym^{k}\widetilde{\mathrm{Ai}}_{n}
.

Proof.

The first statement (weight estimates) follows from Proposition
4.7
. For the second statement, we first prove the results for
\Sym^{k}\widetilde{\mathrm{Ai}}_{n}
by applying Corollaries
5.14
and
5.22
as follows. For
?=\varnothing,{\mathrm{c}},{\mathrm{mid}}
, we note that

\mathrm{H}_{?}^{r}(\mathbb{A}^{\!k},\textstyle\frac{1}{n+1}\sum_{i=1}^{k}x_{i}^{n+1})=\begin{cases}\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\frac{1}{n+1}\,x^{n+1})^{\otimes k}&\text{if $r=k$},\\
0&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

by the argument used in
[
7
, Ex. A.22]
. Furthermore, the exponential mixed Hodge structure
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!1},\frac{1}{n+1}\,x^{n+1})
is pure of weight
1
and of rank
n
according to Section
5.b
, and belongs to
\EMHS^{\widehat{\mu}}
. On the other hand, arguing in a way similar to
[
7
, Ex. A.20]
, we have the short exact sequences in
\EMHS
with a commutative square

(6.21)

\begin{array}[]{c}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 5.5pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 21.1491pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{c}}^{k+1}(\mathbb{A}^{\!k+1},\widetilde{f}_{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern
